[[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland concerning the 20th anniversary of His Holiness John Paul II's visit to the Sejm and the Senate. On 11 June 1999, the visit of the Holy Father John Paul II to the Seym and the Senate took place. It was the first visit of the successor of St. Peter in the building of the national parliament. For us Poles, this event has gained both a symbolic and historical dimension. It confirmed that the direction of political change followed by the fall of communism was right. During the meeting with parliamentarians, John Paul II gave a message to the solemn Assembly of Members and Senators: 'Today, in this place, we are somehow aware of the fundamental role of a just legal order in a democratic country, the foundation of which always and everywhere should be a man and the full truth about a man, his inalienable rights and rights of the entire community, whose name is the Nation'. The words spoken by the Holy Father are still alive and precious to us, a moral signpost for the whole country. This is also the fundamental declaration: 'Poland has the full right to participate in the overall process of progress and development of the world, especially of Europe. The integration of Poland with the European Union has been supported by the Holy See from the outset'. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, on the 20th anniversary of John Paul II's visit to the Sejm and the Senate, pays tribute to the Holy Father and wishes to emphasize that the Polish Pope remains the current model for shaping relations - based on truth, love and peace in contemporary society. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u39	We proceed with the examination of item 3 of the agenda: the Extraordinary Commission's report on the government draft law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and certain other laws (prints Nos 3236 and 3423). I ask Mr Grzegorz Lorek to present a report to the committee, and I ask your Members to remain silent in the Chamber. [[Game in the O.R.]] You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u40	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codifications, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and certain other laws, prints Nos 3236 and 3423. Marshal of the Sejm pursuant to Art. 87. 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for consideration. The Extraordinary Commission discussed the draft at its meeting on 9 May. I would like to remind you that this project concerns a very important issue - the need to introduce a new type of capital company, so-called, into the Polish legal order. a simple public limited company, PSA, which results from the analysis of the legal environment and functioning in Poland so-called. start-ups and is a response to the demands of this environment and business submitted to the resort. The aim of the project is to create a legal framework for dynamic start-ups development. Start-ups are usually young companies looking for a niche market. Their activities are often based on innovative ideas, new technologies and human capital, which faces new challenges. Thanks to technological advantage they have the potential for very rapid growth, but the current legal forms of business activity are anachronistic for them. It should be pointed out that business activity in Poland faces new challenges. This is driven by the dynamic development of technology and the increasing importance of innovation. The potential of Polish start-ups is huge. Already in 2016, it was estimated that if favourable conditions were created for the development of such projects, the sector could generate over 2200 million added value in 2024 and create around 50,000. jobs, which translates into household income at an estimated level of PLN 757 million. Polish investors and experts are appreciated today not only in Europe but also in Silicon Valley. Poland is seventh in the world among the most friendly start-ups and second in terms of overall investment conditions. This law is necessary and necessary for the further development of this branch. Instead of trying to reform the construction of existing capital companies, solutions have been adopted to provide an alternative to existing legislation. This is due to certain legal constraints, the extent of the necessary changes and the safety of trading. PSA is based on two basic assumptions. The first is the simplicity in the practice of the company's operation, i.e. the easy establishment, operation and possible liquidation of PSA. The second assumption is flexibility - the proposed rules do not restrict the freedom of shareholders where this is not necessary for the sake of the safety of trading and the protection of creditors or minority shareholders. The formal requirements related to the establishment, operation and liquidation of the company have been limited to a minimum. During the work, the committee examined 16 amendments. Amendment 3, which regulates the administrative execution of PSA shares dematerialised, i.e. registration in the shareholder register, can be considered the most important. The reasons for this amendment are as follows: These issues were dealt with in the same way as in the draft, adopted by the Economic Committee of the Council of Ministers, which provides for compulsory dematerialisation of the shares of the public limited liability company and the limited liability company. An important amendment is contained in Amendment 13, which provides the basis for covering the professional secrecy of the data collected in the shareholder register. It's a change in the article. 147 Act on Trading in Financial Instruments. On the other hand, of the 13 amendments tabled at the committee meeting, amendment 9 is important, which solves the problem of the impossibility of adopting resolutions on the amendment of the company's contract if the indicated place where the general meeting is to take place is abroad. In this case, it will also be necessary to indicate at least one meeting place in the territory of the Republic of Poland in the company's agreement. Most of the amendments were clear and cross-cutting any doubts as to the content of the provisions. Several amendments, by wording, unless it has been explicitly decided that the question may be settled differently in the company's contract. These are amendments 4 and 5 from the amendments tabled in the subcommittee and amendment 3 from the amendments tabled in the committee. The draft was also adapted to the changes in the law due to amendments 15 and 16 tabled in the subcommittee. On behalf of the committee, I propose that this report be adopted and that this law be further positive. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u41	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Grzegorz Lorek, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u42	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the Government's draft law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and certain other laws, print Nos 3236 and 3423. Today, we are discussing a very important draft law that responds to the challenges of changing the legal environment of doing business based primarily on new technologies and innovation. The proposed changes are initiated by the start-up environment. The implementation of the changes was initiated by analyses and discussions already undertaken in 2016. The White Paper on Innovation of September 2016 confirmed that innovation should be supported inter alia. changes in the legal environment of companies. During the work on the Law, opinions were gathered both in the field of trade law specialists and lawyers of practitioners as well as potential users of the proposed legislation, including representatives of start-ups and investors. PARP also conducted surveys. 73% the subjects who took part in these studies believe that a new legal formula is needed for start-ups. In September 2016, a team of experts was set up, which prepared recommendations for a simple public limited liability company, PSA. This team was composed of prominent representatives of trade law sciences and lawyers working on a daily basis, among others. Start-ups. Instead of attempting to reform the rules of existing capital companies, the project proposes the creation of a new formula for a commercial company. This is due to certain legal constraints, the extent of the necessary changes and the safety of trading. The envisaged solutions are intended to provide an alternative to existing legal structures. This was the way in which regulation was decided for a number of reasons. Firstly, operations should not be carried out on hundreds of thousands of entities. In Poland there are more than 450,000. companies in the O.O. - that's over 84% all commercial companies - and around 12 thousand. public limited liability companies. For many classic businesses that are not based on innovation or human capital, today's capital companies are a tool that works in practice. Secondly, by making profound changes to these companies, all existing entities should be required to adapt their structures to the new rules, e.g. to liquidate the share capital. Even if we carefully assume that the costs of such activities, e.g. changes to the company's contract will be minimal, for existing companies. this would generate a global cost of over PLN 680 million. A better proportionate way to implement the project is therefore to create a new legal formula and to leave companies that today operate in other forms, to decide whether or not they want to use it, e.g. transform into PSA. In Europe, business formulas dedicated to innovative companies are being created. A good example is the Slovak simple joint stock company, which has been operating with our neighbours since 1 January 2017, or the French simplified joint stock company SAS, which is very popular in France. In most countries of the European Union, a capital company with a minimum initial contribution may be established, e.g. 1 euro. The single market offers the opportunity to compete in the legal environment with other countries of the European Union. The groundbreaking importance of this bill is enormous, because we cannot fall behind, although an inspiration to the concept PSA are the needs of start-ups, this new tool will be available to any company for which the traditional organisational framework is too flexible or too complex. PSA is based on two basic assumptions. The first is simplicity in the practice of the company's operation, i.e. its easy establishment and operation. The second is the lack of formal and financial barriers at start-up - it is enough to have share capital of PLN 1 and the establishment of the company will be possible in the system of registration of the company online in 24 hours. Instead of PSA's share capital, equity is created. Shares do not form part of it, do not have a nominal value. Shares can be covered for work, services, while maintaining a key influence on decisions in the company, thanks to a special type of preference of shares, so-called. founding shares, investors can also avoid losing their leading position at the capital search stage. One-man management is enough, there is no obligation to create a supervisory board. It is also possible to create one body in which management and supervision are combined, i.e. the board of directors. We believe that these proposals are going in the right direction. A number of amendments were taken into account during the committee's work. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I strongly recommend continuing to proceed with the bill and make two amendments. Amendment 1. to Article. 300 § 2 and Amendment 2. 2 of the Law of 17 February Code of Civil Procedure.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u60	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! One such issue, which I would sum up, is one such issue - why a new type of company, why not changes or reforms of a limited liability company. And the answer - a limited liability company for today is the most popular corporate vehicle. In Poland, these are hundreds, thousands of companies, living economic organisms that have concluded contracts that somehow function - better, worse, but functioning. This uncertainty can, of course, be even very measurable, because the cost to which the rapporteur has referred here is due to the need to change the agreement of the company, i.e. to visit a notary and then to submit an application to a registered court. This is only the most measurable cost, because there is also a cost related simply to uncertainty about a very important change in the rules of the game, at a time when, as I say, hundreds of thousands of entrepreneurs play in this form all the time, the form of the company. That's why we haven't decided on these changes. In turn, proposing a new form has such an advantage that - please pay attention - the choice will always belong to the entrepreneur, he will decide. And that is kind of the main reason for whether the main motivation to apply a new solution, not to change significantly - I repeat that these are not minor changes - existing companies from o.o. This is how we get full regulation. I'm not saying, and I never said k.s.h. It's pretty simple. Take a k.s.h., read it, see what a simple bill it is. Of course it's not. Trade law is complicated matter, people make habilitation out of it, professors argue about how to interpret a given issue. This is very difficult matter, but within this difficult matter you can create a corporate vehicle, which will, let's say, be more complicated in operation, require more costs, larger enterprises - such a vehicle is e.g. public limited company, the truth, the most complicated - and you can create something simpler like e.g. public limited liability company. On the one hand, a simple public limited liability company is to be a capital company with all the characteristics of capital companies, but on the other hand, the simplest form of these corporate capital vehicles. I think we have achieved this goal, although, as I say, I am aware that we are not talking about a civil company, that it is a capital company. And now the third line. I think it is the most important one, it has appeared in many questions and speeches, it is a question of marketing safety. Here, first of all, I would ask this question: What does it mean - because we say so - that some company is safe? On the one hand, let us take the European benchmark. Ladies and gentlemen, in the UK, in Ireland, in the Netherlands, it could even be a company with zero share capital. In France, Germany, Italy - with 1 euro. Countries where capital requirements are minimal or no, especially if they look at economic potential, in the European Union - I would say so boldly - there is already a majority. It's not like we're inventing, discovering America. No, we are rather trying, within our regulatory environment, to match a certain European standard. Let's look at the Polish benchmark, not the European one, but the Polish one. Since 2008 or 2009 the minimum share capital in the company z o.o. It's 5,000. zł. Here's the question of somebody going to the bank, they're gonna have to take extra collateral. Here, in this room with your hand on your heart, tell me if someone has $5,000. zł share capital, it will not need additional collateral from the bank. He'll get a loan for doing business. It's obvious that today, as far as the 5,000 are concerned. zł in the case of the company, this is, let's say, a formality. The legislators probably didn't have the guts to go down to that zloty, because there was also this concept of the company for the zloty, and it stayed with the 5,000. zł. What's that enough for? Tell me what it's enough for when someone starts doing business in the form of a company from O.O. And how does that safeguard the creditor's interests? But look at PSA now. We have here a real mechanism that binds capital, and it's not about share capital, because it's not called share capital, it's about reserve. This mechanism binds the amount of the reserve - i.e. simply the money, the cash that has to be held in the company - to the amount of liabilities determined year by year and therefore the reserves held in PSA will have to be linked to the real amount of liabilities. In my opinion, this is a much more real mechanism than the mechanism, I would say, so very formal - not to say more - that is, the one with the share capital of 5 000. zł. That's another line. Members of the Management Board PSA, of course, is responsible for the company's obligations subsidiaryly, i.e. after the execution of the company's assets proves ineffective. And the next thing I know, it's a transformation. Of course, there will be the possibility of transforming into k.s.h. it is possible to convert one capital company into another. However, please bear in mind that every time we transform according to the Commercial Companies Code - here we do not make any changes - it is preceded by such a special procedure: communication procedure, which among others. it takes a few months, from memory I say something about 6 months, and he wouldn't take his head off for it, and it's a procedure in which creditors can demand collateral, i.e. if the creditors consider that transformation e.g. from the company O.O. a simple public limited liability company is a victim to them, they have the possibility to apply in the course of this procedure for the protection of their claims. As I say, we are not making any changes in this area of trade law. This is a mechanism that already works. Therefore, both because of this procedure and because of what I mentioned earlier, i.e. because of this mechanism of maintaining the reserve directly linked to each size of liabilities on the company's side and with the liability of the board members, such as in the company's o.o. If, on this basis, PSA is safe for creditors, and it is certainly safer than the company's, for sure. And it is certainly safer than those companies in countries, countries of the European Union, which I have mentioned, which operate either on zero capital or on capital of EUR 1. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u62	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I present on behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications a report on the government draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act and certain other laws, print Nos 3137 and 3457. The purpose of the presentation, as indicated by the Council of Ministers, is a comprehensive change in the civil procedure aimed at improving the system of management of the case, while at the same time protecting the rights of the parties and improving the conditions for those involved in the process. The implementation of these objectives is to be achieved through changes to civil procedural regulations in nine areas: preparation of the case for investigation, prevention of abuse of procedural law, evidence, justification of judgments and appeals, proceedings in economic matters and other separate proceedings, general improvement of proceedings, judicial costs as well as implementing and transitional provisions. Marshal of the Sejm at the 77th meeting of the Sejm on 20 February 2019 pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for consideration. Written opinions were presented by: National Chamber of Legal Advisers, Supervisory Board of ZUS, Polish Chamber of Experts, National Bank of Poland, Financial Supervision Commission and spokesperson for small and medium-sized enterprises. The Extraordinary Commission on Codification Changes at its meeting on 21 February referred the draft to the Permanent Sub-Committee on Civil Law Amendment, which concluded its work on 11 April 2019 after six meetings. At the meeting of the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codification on 14 May, the report on the work of the subcommittee was presented by its President, Mr Waldemar Buda. The meeting was also attended by Łukasz Piebiak, Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Justice, together with colleagues, Paulina Tronowska, specialist in the legal team of the Financial Ombudsman's Office, Anisa Gnacikowska, Deputy Secretary of the Supreme Bar Council, together with colleagues, Anna Suska, representative of the National Council of Legal Counsels, Adrian Zwoliński, expert in the Confederacy of Leviathan, together with colleagues, Jan Pressałek, legislative representative of the Polish Credit Management Union, Anna Wypy-Knieć, representative of the Association of Polish Judges Iustia, Marcin Czugan, Vice President of the Board of the Conference of Financial Enterprises in Poland, as well as employees of the secretariat and advisors of the Extraordinary Commission and legislators from the Legislative Office. A discussion on the project took place at the meeting. All regulations have been systematically discussed. Several dozen amendments have been tabled, including substantive, ordering and legislative amendments. Five Members ultimately voted in favour of the bill, no one opposed it, no one abstained. The Extraordinary Commission for Codification Changes after considering the draft law at its meetings on 21 February and 14 May asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law as proposed in print No 3357. The project responds to social expectations and aims to improve those aspects of the functioning of the courts which Poles most often criticised, thus reducing the length of proceedings and judging on the basis of insufficient evidence, increasing the timeliness of the courts, improving the organisation of courts and ensuring more humane treatment of citizens in courts. These are the intentions of the government and the presented project, but these are also the demands of Law and Justice for almost 20 years, confirmed not only by public opinion polls, but also by daily experience in the field of thousands of meetings with Poles and legal advice given in the offices of parliamentarians and councillors of our formation. In this project there are many solutions that will improve and speed up proceedings, make the civil process more flexible. We rightly place more emphasis on settlement of the dispute, giving judgment as soon as possible after the first hearing, bringing a hearing day after day, preventing and combating procedural obstruction and reducing formalism, e.g. when declaring judgments. It is also valuable that we strengthen the rights of the weaker parties, e.g. by exempting from legal fees the underprivileged and employees of their rights towards employers. In conclusion, the draft after the committee's work is very positive. At the same time, I make eight amendments, mainly complementary and clarifying. You're welcome.

All reforms focused on speeding up have introduced formalism, which unfortunately is at the expense of material truth. The judgment given in the case should be as appropriate as possible to the well-established facts. Also, excessive fiscalism contradicts the justice system. High Izbo! Any change in the model of civil proceedings should be preceded by a deep, substantive discussion involving representatives of all legal professions and scientific backgrounds, and the project itself should be thoroughly refined. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u72	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, I would like to present our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act and some other laws, prints no. 3137 and 3457. In fact, I am willing to agree with the authors of the project, with the rapporteur, that these are changes that were expected by Poles, are expected by Poles. It is only regrettable that it was only at the end of the 8th term of the Sejm that this materialized in the form of an amendment to the Code of Civil Procedure. There was a time for controversial projects concerning the Supreme Court, the Constitutional Court and other institutions of fundamental importance in the area of justice to be undertaken immediately, immediately at the beginning of the term of office, while these real, real cases have only been dealt with today. This is undoubtedly regrettable. The project actually introduces several elements, which can be called a thorough reform of civil proceedings. The aim of the authors is to streamline, simplify and speed up procedures. A large part of the case is to be settled at the first hearing. This objective is to be achieved by reducing procedural obstructions, which is indeed a problem for the justice system. The use by attorneys of various legal loopholes only to gain time is the peril of every court. This makes it difficult for the other side to investigate justice. This is commonly raised by Poles in various situations and therefore the project introduces sanctions for abuse of procedural law. A judge who finds that another complaint in the same case is only a delay game will be able to decide to double the costs of the trial or interest. In addition, the court will be able to leave a number of applications submitted for obstruction without consideration. This is to be served, among others. introducing an obligation to respond in writing to a lawsuit. If it is not possible to close the proceedings at the first hearing, the judge will immediately have to set several consecutive periods of trial immediately following each other so that there are no months' breaks between them. The proposed rules shorten the way for citizens to the courts: cases are to take place near their place of residence. That's good, too. The possibility of extending proceedings has also been limited, e.g. by constantly pressing complaints. New solutions also guarantee the objectivity of proceedings - automatic exclusion of judges and entire compositions when their impartiality can be questioned. There will also be no pronouncements in an empty courtroom. Right now, the judge has to do it even when no one's stood up. The draft also assumes that in the written justifications of the judgments it will be sufficient to outline the substance of the problem, which will significantly relieve judges. This party will determine what justification it expects: full or partial. In addition, the possibility to make written statements in all civil court proceedings has been extended. One element of the reform proposed by the government is also the exemption from legal fees for the less prosperous and workers who gain their rights from employers. Discounts have also been offered for the victims of unfair sellers: fees will be reduced by 2/3 if the injured person tries to resolve the dispute amicably before bringing an action. The victims of the banks, the franchise, will pay a maximum of 1 000 for bringing the case. zł, and now it can be tens of thousands of zlotys. Reasonable doubts are raised by the planned return to the fast track for entrepreneurs. This will be a much more formalized procedure, with a tightened prognosis. Generally, from a social point of view, the solutions contained in the project should be considered appropriate. There are quite significant doubts, [[♪ The bell ♪]] on the issue of judicial costs, as a general rule, but in the course of further work there may be a chance to face this problem. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

As regards the question of Mrs Dolniak, the charge of set-off, the possibility of procedural recourse to the charge of set-off, as you are well aware, has already existed in the Polish legal system, in the old separate proceedings in economic matters, even at some point has been tightened up, i.e. what is now proposed in the submission, is a return to something that was good. Why was that good? Not because, as it is said here, we artificially force people to multiply the number of processes, because if we have mutual settlements, there are situations in which two processes will have to take place by limiting the charge of deduction. The point is that the charge of deduction has been used over the years to block proceedings against claims that do not raise greater doubts. The point was that when it was necessary to pay, very often to invent the claim which is presented to be deducted, to build it into a legend, to submit to it evidence requests in the form of witnesses and experts, and to force the court and the adversary to make this procedure spread, because there is an expert opinion, because it is still possible to apply for exemption from costs in this opinion, etc., etc. Your marshal was a judge, I was also a judge, I was an economic judge in Warsaw, so I really know what I'm talking about, I met with it every day in the Warsaw business courts where I ruled. And it was just a situation that caused the length of the proceedings, which caused that someone who had an undisputed amount of money, because of such procedural smartness, because otherwise it could not be defined, could not obtain this money by judicial procedure, because on the other hand there was quite a good lawyer or legal counsel who created the legend. as regards the existence of a claim submitted for deduction. In fact, that legal situation and today's legal situation have caused the length of proceedings, causing the inability to claim legitimate claims. Since the main motive for this submission is to eliminate the obstruction of proceedings, to eliminate all that slows down the procedure, it was impossible not to go back to the previous solution, which just limited the possibility of taking advantage of the charge of set-off. That's what kept the trials going. Of course, you can probably find situations where the charge of deduction wasn't a destructive charge, but a real one, but it's all a matter of scale. It is not unusual, however, that procedural law introduces certain limitations as regards the possibility of using material law charges, as is the case with the charge of deduction. This is absolutely in line with the general idea of this code. The point is to give judges and courts tools that will enable them to overcome influence efficiently, to deal with human affairs, business matters. Thus, the possibility of making unfettered use of the charge of deduction would mean that many good changes that we are proposing here would be offset by ordinary, vulgar procedural shrewdness. Thank you very much. [[And the cost?]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u102	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I will try to address the questions that were asked during the discussion. Mrs Tomaszewska, in drawing up the genesis of the law on the organisation and functioning of pension funds, rightly pointed out that since the provisions of this law were adopted and later the law on the organisation and functioning of pension funds was adopted, much has happened. First of all, Poland became a member of the European Union and had access to, you can say, labour markets of other Member States of the European Union, which resulted in the mobility of workers, taking up work by Polish workers in other Member States of the European Union. The question was whether the termination of the worker's employment relationship or the employee who is a member of the occupational pension scheme meant the need to transfer funds, return funds. This issue is, of course, already regulated in the Employee Pension Schemes Act today, and it can be said that there is no automation here. I mean, these measures are to be at the disposal of a member of the occupational pension scheme when he reaches the age of 60. And this principle, so to speak, still applies, which means that these funds will only be available to the participant when it reaches that age limit. Regarding Mrs Czernow's question about the number of Poles who... She asked about the number of Poles who are working in other Member States of the European Union, but who are also members of the occupational pension scheme. Perhaps I will answer this question a little differently, because I can give you data about... in the course of the discussion, and this information was provided here. In fact, this bill that we are discussing today concerns, firstly, persons who are participants in occupational pension schemes, but, secondly, this second element is very important, the point is that they are occupational pension schemes carried out in the form of an occupational pension fund. Hence, the bill provides for changes in two regulations, in two laws, i.e. it is in the Act on the organisation and functioning of pension funds and, on the other hand, in the Act on occupational pension schemes. The latter law has a slightly shorter history because it was passed in 2004. Such schemes, which meet both conditions, i.e. they are occupational pension schemes and are carried out in the form of an occupational pension fund, at the moment we have two in Poland. These are the Workers' Pension Fund Nowy Świat and the Workers' Pension Fund Orange Poland. As far as the number of participants in these programmes is concerned, this is about 32 000. persons. As regards the amount of funds managed by both occupational pension societies, this is a envelope of PLN 1 808 931 137.30. I would like to point out that this law only applies to this category of persons. This law gives these people the opportunity to work in another Member State of the European Union without, you can say, damage to savings accumulated so far in the occupational pension scheme. She also pointed out that in Poland, in the context of, above all, employee capital plans or the entry into force of the Act on Employee Capital Plans, the interest of Poles in saving under additional programmes, plans to raise funds to be used after retirement age or around the age of retirement. I confirm that such interest is indeed being observed at the moment. The number of new occupational pension schemes that have been created recently is around 300. As regards the issue of the impact of this law on the labour market, in general, the assessment of the effects of the regulation annexed to the draft law indicates that this law will not affect the labour market. However, I would like to talk about some benefits. First of all, the benefits for these 32 000. participants in occupational pension schemes are the benefits of greater security of measures, but also the possibility of taking up work in another Member State of the European Union without, as it were, interrupting the process of saving in occupational pension schemes. Mr Raś asked me about the discrepancies, and you also paid attention to this during the discussion. So I'm going to say that the work on this bill has been going on for a long time. Indeed, the deadline for implementation, which was written in the Act itself, is 13 January 2019. But I would like to say that the legislative process was launched on 21 August 2018, so that, I repeat, the work lasted quite long. This work also included the process of agreeing with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, agreement on compliance with European Union law and arrangements on the issue of the full implementation of this Directive in national law. I would like to say that the opinion of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs on the full compatibility of this bill is decisive for us. I state that we have no doubts here about the possible discrepancy with the provisions of the Directive. I think I answered those questions you asked during the discussion. I would like to ask the High House again for further work on this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u106	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on a government draft law amending the Waste Act and some other laws. The draft is contained in print No 3412 and the committee's report in print No 3464. This draft law clarifies the provisions on the functioning of the database on products and packaging and on waste management, called waste data base in a nutshell. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: In particular, the Law of 14 December 2012 on Waste, but also the Law on Cleanliness and Order in Municipalities, the Act on Environmental Law, the Act on Obligations of Entrepreneurs in the Management of Certain Wastes and on Product Fee, the Act on Batteries and Battery, the Act on Packaging and Packaging Waste Management, the Act on Used Electrical and Electronic Equipment, and the Act on Amendment of the Waste Act and some other Acts and the latest Act on Amendment of the Act on Personal Evidence and Certain Other Acts are amended. High Izbo! The waste data base following the changes made in November 2017 has three modules: the waste database register, the reporting and the waste records module. The first one, the entry in the register, was launched on 23 January 2018 and subsequent modules will be launched from 1 January 2020. Then only electronic handling of applications for registration will be possible, and the operation of the system for entities included in this database will be in electronic form, using an individual account. It will then be possible for traders to abolish the obligation to attach original documents to the application, such as proof of payment of the registration fee, certificates issued by banks keeping accounts for the organisation of the recovery of electrical and electronic equipment to the payment of the amount to cover the share capital of the recovery organisation, as well as a document confirming the payment of the financial security. It will also be possible to provide a database of waste documents confirming the collection, recovery and recycling of packaging waste, waste electrical and electronic equipment, waste batteries and accumulators and waste from lubricating oils and tyres. In the provisions amending the Waste Act, contained in Article. 1 of the project in question, models of registration forms for reports and documents that have so far been specified in the Waste Act regulations will be replaced by specifying the scope of the information of these forms and documents directly in the Waste Act, i.e. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 1, Article Article 67 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4, Article 73. 2, Article 74. 2, Article 74a, Art. Article 75 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 - those are the provisions for which further amendments to Article are made. 1. These changes, which meet the requirements of the regulations I quote, will only be made available through the waste database system. Amendment 28. in Article. 1 contains, in turn, an amendment to the expenditure rule covering the costs of producing and maintaining the waste database in 2019 and 2028. This amount is broken down into individual amounts, but in total it is 45 444 thousand. zł. Similarly, the law on maintaining cleanliness and order in municipalities will be amended. Amendments shall be made to Article. 9n paragraph. 3, Article 9th paragraph. 3, Article 9nb paragraph. 3, Article Paragraph 9o. 3, Article 9oa, Art. 9p paragraph. 1, Article Article 9q (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 shall apply. 3, Article Paragraph 9s. 3, Article 9t. These changes aim to effectively control the management of municipal waste in municipalities in terms of the amount of such waste, the way in which it is managed. In addition, keeping records of municipal waste reporting in electronic form, i.e. through the waste database, it will enable both a thorough analysis of the situation in this area and an assessment of the achievement of the recycling targets set by European legislation. Always an electronic form of document circulation facilitates all analytical activities. High Izbo! In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 4, 5, 6 and 7 also introduce provisions extending the waste database to include a module covering the collection, recovery and recycling of waste batteries and accumulators, packaging waste, waste electrical and electronic equipment and waste from lubricating oils and tyres. The revised rules will allow the identification of entities entering the waste database on the basis of the PESEL number, which is relevant for small waste producers, i.e. entities such as natural persons carrying out an activity from which the income does not exceed 50% the amount of the minimum remuneration, or e.g. flat-rate farmers who produce packaging waste or related to the self-service of machinery or equipment which are obliged to keep records of waste and are therefore required to obtain an entry in the register as a waste producer. These small waste producers do not always have a tax identification number and, as you know, a law on the rules of registration and identification of taxpayers and payers requires that it be either a NIP number or a PESEL number. So these small waste producers will be able to be identified on the basis of the PESEL number. These changes are contained in Article. 9 and 10 of the draft I am discussing here in the report of the Environment Committee. Further articles of the project, i.e. Article 2 11.20, contain transitional and adaptation provisions. Art. The provisions concerning changes to the register, the keeping of waste records and the method of drawing up and reporting for which the existing provisions apply by the end of 2019, but for the records of waste referred to in Article. Article 67 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Waste Act, the existing provisions will also apply after 2019. In accordance with the transitional provisions set out in Article. The existing rules will apply until the end of 2020 to documents confirming separate recovery and recycling, on the basis of existing waste management laws, on the basis of the Act on batteries and accumulators, the Act on waste electrical and electronic equipment and on the basis of the Act on packaging and packaging waste management. As stated in the article. 21 of the draft, the Act will enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of the provisions contained in the amendments 1 to 3 lit. a, from 4. to 11., from 13. to 16., 18 to 20., 21st lit. A, 22, 23 and 26. These are the changes in the article. 1 which are the exception for the date of entry into force. The provisions in Article will also be an exception. Point 5 of Article 2 8 and 9 which shall enter into force on 1 January 2020 Another exception is the amendments contained in Article. This Regulation shall be binding in its entirety and directly applicable in all Member States. High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u108	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I present the position of the club Law and Justice about the government draft law amending the Waste Act and some other laws, prints No 3412 and 3464. The draft amendment to the Waste Act of 14 December 2012, hereinafter referred to as the Waste Act, results from the need to clarify the provisions relating to the operation of the BDO database on products, packaging and waste management. The project concerns a revision of the expenditure rule concerning the funds of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. The draft law proposes to keep waste records only electronically from 1 January 2020 through an individual BDO account and to refrain from using paper form of documents. The announcement by the voivodship marshal about the activation of the account in the register and about the login and password given to the system will take place electronically. This will be confirmed by the Marshal of the voivodship, either by registering or by listing the information in the register. It is very important to clarify in the case of suppliers of waste for storage and landfill managers. Documents relating to the basic characteristics of waste and the results of the conformity tests will continue to be carried out and transmitted in paper form. Reservation: electronic form of application will not apply to entities, foreign entrepreneurs and other foreign persons involved in economic trade who have not established a branch in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The facilitation for entrepreneurs resulting from the changes is the abolition of the obligation to deliver the originals and to replace them with copies attached to the application for registration. It is also interesting to include documents submitted together with an application for entry in the BDO register within an individual account created for a given entity. The implementation of the BDO electronic reporting module will take place from 1 January 2020 at the latest. A regulation has been added which provides that for a specific type of waste in the BDO system it will be possible to identify their manufacturer and subsequent holders of the waste concerned. The costs of establishing, implementing and maintaining the BDO system will be covered by the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. The possibility of electronic transmission by the BDO system of documents confirming the collection, recovery and recycling of packaging waste, waste from lubricating oils and tyres, waste electrical and electronic equipment and waste batteries and accumulators has been introduced. There is also a transitional provision indicating that the existing waste records will be applied by the end of 2019 as a fundamental change in the way waste records are kept is expected from 1 January 2020. It is very important to align the expenditure rule with the revised timetable for the BDO system, which has been established for a period of 10 years, for the years 2016 to 2025. The draft revised legislation will have a positive impact on the activities of small, medium and large enterprises. These solutions that we are currently discussing are consistent with European Union legislation. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u142	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, High Seym! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure, I have the honour to present to the High Sejm a report on the government draft law amending the Road Traffic Law and the Road Transport Act, print Nos 3456 and 3509. On behalf of the committee, I would like to provide some basic information on the key issues covered by this project, which is implementing the European Directive, in order to signal these issues once again. The draft law amending the Road Traffic Law and the Road Transport Act aims to implement the relevant Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council (EU) of 2014 on the technical roadside inspection of the airworthiness of commercial vehicles moving within the Union and repealing the earlier Directive, of 2000. In order to fully implement Directive 2014/47/EU, it is necessary to amend the Road Traffic Law Act and the Road Transport Act. This was presented in very detail by the representatives of the infrastructure department at the meeting of the Sejm committee. The subject of this regulation is the roadside technical inspection of vehicles designed and constructed for the carriage of more than 9 persons including the driver. These are categories of M2 and M3 vehicles intended for the carriage of goods with a maximum total weight exceeding 3,5 t, trailers with a total weight exceeding 3,5 t and wheeled tractors used on public roads for the purpose of carrying out commercial carriage of goods. In short, the draft law provides for the following most important changes. Firstly, the possibility of carrying out detailed technical roadside inspections of vehicles falling within the scope of that Directive at mobile road inspection stations and at designated points of inspection. Secondly, the introduction of an obligation imposed on the Road Transport Inspection in agreement with the Police Chief Commandant for the planning and organisation of preliminary checks on the technical status of vehicles which are subject to the regulation of that Directive, in such a way that the total number of these checks covers at least 5% each year the total number of such vehicles registered in the territory of the Republic of Poland. We are implementing an article in this regard. 5 Directive on the technical roadside inspection of the airworthiness of vehicles in use and movement within the Union. Thirdly, the introduction of an obligation for controllers and road inspectors to carry out detailed technical checks on appropriate training and qualifications. Fourthly, the extension of the application of the risk assessment system as regards the number and weight of deficiencies detected in the categories of vehicles covered by that Directive. Fifthly, allow for the exchange of information between the Member States of the European Union concerning the finding of serious or dangerous defects or defects in the vehicle leading to the restriction or prohibition of the use of a particular vehicle. Sixthly, the implementation of this Directive will allow the Road Transport Inspections to cooperate with the inspection authorities of other Member States in coordinated roadside inspections of the technical airworthiness of vehicles. I would also like to signal that, in addition, in the draft law authorising the regulation, a point concerning specific conditions for the exercise of road traffic control of vehicles of the State Protection Service, Police, Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Intelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, Central Anti-corruption Office, Border Guard, National Tax Administration used by the Customs and Skarb Service was added to the bill. 10 amendments were tabled during the drafting of the bill. These amendments were taken into account after a rather detailed discussion. Most of the amendments were editorial and detailed in terms of government submission. I would like to thank on behalf of the committee for the good cooperation of the legal services of the Ministry of Infrastructure and the Legal Office of the Sejm, which has served, I think, in a good way to the work of the committee. Today's second reading is taking place and I hope that the Government's submission will be accepted by the High House. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u143	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Grzegorz Wozniak will speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u144	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law on amending the Road Traffic Law and the Road Transport Act, which, as the previous speaker said, aims to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014 on the technical roadside inspection of the airworthiness of commercial vehicles moving within the European Union. The subject of the regulation of the Directive is road technical inspection: vehicles carrying out road transport, goods and persons, inter alia. they are vehicles designed and equipped for the carriage of more than nine persons, including the driver, vehicles designed and adapted for the carriage of cargo with a maximum total mass exceeding 3,5 t, as well as trailers with a maximum total mass exceeding 3,5 t and wheeled tractors principally used on public roads and for commercial additional services, commercial transport of goods. The proposed amendment to the Road Traffic Law introduced an obligation for the driver to have a protocol from the last roadside technical inspection if it was carried out in the case of the vehicle concerned. The obligation for the driver to have a vehicle registered abroad is also introduced and a document confirming that the technical examination has been carried out on the territory of the Republic of Poland. This obligation does not apply to drivers of a new vehicle from the date of its first registration abroad until the date of the first periodic technical test, as new vehicles do not undergo technical tests before the first registration. The project also adds an obligation for the Chief Inspector of Road Transport together with the Chief Police Officer to plan and organise preliminary technical roadside inspections of vehicles listed in the Act in such a way that the total number of inspections per year covers at least 5% the total number of such vehicles registered in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The project also sets out a system of technical roadside inspections including preliminary roadside checks, technical roadside checks and a place where these technical checks would be carried out by mobile roadside inspection stations. These points will be located in places defined jointly by the various services I have already mentioned. The use of a mobile road inspection station will shorten the time of the inspection and reduce the costs associated with the control of road transport operators, as detailed checks will be carried out on-the-spot during the inspection. You won't have to move any more. This bill will increase road safety and help to keep vehicles safe and environmentally acceptable during their use. Road technical checks are a key element for achieving a sustainable high level of airworthiness of commercial vehicles. Attention is often paid, there are often complaints and this comes out during checks that, however, with vehicles in terms of technical condition, it is not exactly as it should be. These checks will also contribute to reducing emissions from vehicles and to preventing unfair competition in road transport related to the different levels of control applied by individual European Union countries. In view of the validity of this law and the factors I have said that will change and improve, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I request that this law be adopted. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u145	Thank you. And I invite Mr Stanisław Żmijan, the Civic Platform Club, the Civic Coalition. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u146	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club, I present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Road Traffic Law Act and the Road Transport Act, Seym Printing Nos 3456 and 3509. But before I go to the point, please allow the High House to congratulate Mr Jerzy Polachk on taking up office as Chairman of the Committee on Infrastructure. I wish you fruitful work, fruitful management of the committee. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The draft law implements a directive of the European Parliament and of the Council into our law, a very important directive, as the guidelines contained therein provide tools to coordinate the technical control of vehicles throughout the European Community's road network, of course after implementation by the Member States. The implementation of the directive into our law has several great meanings. In particular, it is important because it gives the opportunity to improve safety on the Polish road network because of the possibility - through these checks - of eliminating vehicles that do not hold technical standards and technical requirements which must meet vehicles that can move on Polish roads. Of course, this applies to the whole European network, because the regulations contained in this project provide an opportunity to exchange information between countries, so that they are regulations of great importance. The second reason is, of course, the possibility of reducing emissions, harmful environmental impacts. We know our problems in many cities due to smog, due to exhaust emissions. This is also a very important regulation. There is also an important reason, namely the possibility of maintaining or eliminating unfair competition between traders. After all, these passenger and freight transports are carried out by traders who make use of this option and do not necessarily bear the full costs of maintaining a high technical standard for these vehicles. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u164	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law on the labelling of products produced without the use of genetically modified organisms as free from these organisms, print Nos 3262 and 3446. On 6 March 2019 the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development. The Commission, following its first reading and consideration of this proposal at its meetings on 14 May and 11 June this year. asks the Sejm to adopt the attached bill. The government has prepared this project, responding to societal needs, the demands of many organisations and environments which have demanded the labelling of non-GMO food so that the consumer can consciously choose the products he buys in the store. The GMO-free labelling is also complementary to the agricultural minister's programme to increase the share of national plant protein without GMOs in feed production. So far, despite several successive governments, the feed industry with the dominant participation of foreign companies is based on imported soya meal, which is almost 100% is genetically modified. Allowing producers to produce and market food of animal origin labelled as GMO-free will increase the demand for national feed produced on the basis of national sources of GMO-free protein. The draft law provides for voluntary labelling of products which will be described as GMO-free. These may be products of animal origin, from animals or from feed-fed animals without GMOs or plant origin. A compound food containing at least 50% may also be labelled feedingstuffs may also be labelled as ingredients eligible for the labelling and, which is very important. The draft law also sets out rules for labelling food and feed, introducing uniform graphical signs - it will be found in the Minister of Agriculture's regulation - so that food consumers have no doubt that the product is non-GMO or contains GMOs. Possible technically unavoidable thresholds for GMO admixtures in food and feed have also been established. For food, this threshold is very low, at 0.1%. It's also low for feed, because it's 0.9% here. Similar solutions have been adopted in other countries of the European Union where such labelling has been introduced. This is Germany, France, Slovenia, Italy, Austria. So that is a fairly common solution. The Act also sets out a delegation to the Minister for Agriculture to set minimum transitional periods, withdrawal periods for feed without GMOs. In addition, the draft law provides for obligations for operators placing on the market products labelled as GMO-free. The most important responsibility, of course, will be to perform laboratory tests. The manufacturer will also be required to keep documents proving that the tests have been carried out. The draft law also states that products from other countries that use GMO-free labelling will be recognised in Poland under a mutual trust clause. In addition, the draft specifies that the bodies controlling the implementation of the law will be the Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection and the Veterinary Inspection. Penalties have also been laid down for possible failure to comply with the provisions of the Act. I would like to inform the High House that the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development has adopted three amendments tabled by the chairman of the committee, Mr Sachaika. These three amendments sharpen the penalties. The government project originally provided for lower penalties, now more severe penalties. As these amendments were accepted, notabene adopted another amendment to Article. 4 - the committee requested the Ministry of Foreign Affairs to submit by 13 June an opinion on the compatibility of the Commission's report on prints Nos 3262 and 3446 and in particular on the obligation to notify again. The draft states that the Act will enter into force on 1 January 2020. Its reception is expected by most Poles. This will also have a very positive impact on the development of Polish agriculture. Therefore, on behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I would like to ask the High Sejm to approve the government bill contained in prints 3262 and 3446. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u166	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the governmental draft law on the labelling of products produced without the use of genetically modified organisms as free from these organisms, print No. 3262. The government's proposal for labelling products produced without the use of genetically modified organisms is based on requests from social organisations, consumer organisations and some producers. In the light of the public debate on GMOs, which regularly appears with every legislative initiative in this area, calls are also intensified to give consumers the right to choose when buying food products because of information on whether a product has a genetic modification and whether GMOs have been used in the production process. This is driven by continuing uncertainty and concern about the long-term effects of genetic modification on human health. Non-governmental organisations regularly carry out public opinion polls and campaigns to produce results indicating that 65% Poles are in favour of banning GMO cultivation. The Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development in October 2017 received more than 1 000. calls for the introduction of a single GMO-free mark which would indicate how and from what ingredients food is available to consumers. In view of the fact that consumers are demanding labelling of GMO-free products, as well as the strengthening of government actions to increase the domestic production of plant protein for feed purposes, and the brand of Polish food, a draft law has been drawn up which will regulate the labelling of products as GMO-free. Products to be covered by such a labelling scheme shall be single and multi-component products of plant origin which have equivalent genetically modified products authorised on the market of the European Union. This includes maize, rape, soya, sugar beet, which are not genetically modified, but are conventional equivalents of genetically modified products. The next group are products of animal origin: meat, milk, eggs, cheese. They come from animals or from feed-fed animals without GMOs. Examples are products that have been produced in animal production using e.g. non-GM plant protein of native production. These conditions have been the basis for creating GMO-free labelling, which has been using several European Union countries for nearly 10 years. The initiative of the Polish government is in this respect consistent with the actions of these countries. Food of animal origin may be labelled as GMO-free if it comes from animals or from animals fed during the withdrawal period prior to their procurement and the feeding process of GMOs will be documented. In the case of food of animal origin, labelling free of GMOs is permitted, e.g. meat, carcases, eggs, milk as well as processed food containing ingredients other than products of animal origin, e.g. soy protein is often used in the production of meat. In the case of processed foods, which are also made up of other ingredients, these ingredients must comply with the requirements of the Act so that the food can be labelled as GMO-free. The following labelling principles are proposed in the draft law: a graphic symbol and a possible word expression. The basic marking on packages or labels of all products shall be a graphic symbol, the model of which shall be laid down in a regulation issued by the Minister responsible for Agriculture and the Minister responsible for Agricultural Markets. It may be accompanied by the term "no GMO' in the case of plant products or by the term "produced without the use of GMOs' in products of animal origin. The compliance checks will be carried out by the Agricultural and Food Articles Trade Quality Inspection, Veterinary Inspection and Commercial Inspection, in accordance with their respective tasks. The draft law regulates sanctions concerning deficiencies in the placing on the market of products labelled as GMO-free. After an in-depth examination of the draft law presented by the government at committee meetings and after the club discussion, having regard to the absolute right of the consumer to make an informed choice while standing at the same time, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that conscious choice is one of human freedom, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the draft law submitted by the government attached to print report 3446 and will vote in favour of its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In fact, we have been eating such food in Poland for at least a dozen years, because, as we must say, every animal product produced in Poland, meat, milk, eggs or other products, is produced on the basis of genetically modified soybeans. Today Poles are stuffed with this food. The public, I agree with all the predecessors, does not accept genetically modified food. He wants to have food that doesn't involve GMOs. It's a good trend. Other trends are also good, e.g. trends to reach for food produced with less use of plant protection products or fertilisers. There are even special quality programs that talk about, for example. there were no residues of such preparations in plant products. The most classic, farthest-reaching trend is organic production, which in Poland and other European countries is developing in terms of production, and it is increasingly seen on store shelves. An increasing number of consumers are reaching for such food. It's the safest. Observing the trends that occur in Poland, the wealth of society for several dozens of years, for 12 years, we see an increase in interest in healthy food. It's an imprecise term, but it involves, among other things. for food free from genetically modified organisms. Of course, a number of questions arise when discussing this law. They deal with this in an excellent way and relieve the burden on the state administration. Production of organic food in Poland, despite the decline in the number of hectares that are covered, is growing. Very good. We can see clearly that organic production is starting to produce, not just to receive subsidies. For this reason, it is worth supporting this law to give the consumer the right to choose. The consumer is not yet fully aware of whether this food is harmful to him or not. He feels that it can potentially be harmful, and he prefers to reach for products he can be sure of. The consumer, going to the store and choosing a specific product, should receive specific information: whether it contains GMOs or not. That's the demand. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u174	Dear Marshal! Dear Ministers, High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the government's draft labeling of products produced without the use of genetically modified organisms as free from these organisms, prints 3262 and 3446. The governmental draft law on the labelling of products produced without the use of genetically modified organisms as free from those organisms sets out the principles of voluntary labelling of food and feed as GMO-free and the obligations of operators placing such labelled products on the market. Three government inspections will be responsible for the control system: Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection, Veterinary Inspection and Commercial Inspection. The proposed regulations are intended to limit self-conclusion as to the inclusion of GMO information on products. As GMO-free products can be labelled under regulated conditions. In the case of feedingstuffs, the presence of genetically modified ingredients may not exceed 0.9% , in the case of food - 0,1% Whereas in the case of food derived from feed-free animals without GMOs, this applies to feedingstuffs which contain less than 0.9% GMOs. It is worth noting that EU legislation already in force requires information on the GMO content to be included in products that have more than 0.9% genetically modified ingredients. According to the project presented, only products of plant origin for which EU law permits the GMO content but does not contain it may be labelled as GMO-free. If the product does not have an acceptable equivalent of genetically modified, it cannot be labelled as GMO-free. In the case of mixed compound products, ingredients of animal origin and ingredients of plant origin which may be labelled in accordance with the Act as GMO-free must be at least 50% the product. This law, although it can introduce high standards of Polish food, can also be risky for it. This concerns competition with foreign EU products labelled as GMO-free, but on other legal principles. Agricultural producers and food producers will be able to add marketing value to their products, paying for laboratory tests for genetic modification, labelling, certification, purchase of non-genetically modified feed. This will certainly translate into the prices of these products. It also raises the question of how many manufacturers will be able to go through this process and obtain the right to label their products as GMO-free. Following the implementation of this project on the Polish market, products will be labelled voluntarily as GMO-free, non-labelled products and products subject to mandatory labelling of the presence of GMOs, which results directly from EU legislation. This may cause chaos, given that voluntary labelling only applies to products of plant origin in which maize, rapeseed, soya and sugar beet are made up, but not so many consumers know it. The GMO-free password will certainly have a strong impact. But will this project result in greater consumer awareness? We don't know. The Modern Posel Club, despite these project shortcomings, will vote in favour of the project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u192	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! [[Everybody said that, everybody said that.]] Of course, if we... [[There is no evidence, sir, there is no evidence that it is harmful.]] So that is what I would like to say about it, sir. The mechanism for the harmfulness of GMOs is not that if we consume genetically modified plants, we, as consumers, will also be subjected to genetic mutations, i.e. that this cross-border gene will pass from plants to humans or animals that, for example, would consume plants, because that is not the essence of this modification. The Minister said that the essence of genetic modification in most cases lies in the fact that plants are implanted with a gene for resistance to the active ingredient Roundup, i.e. glyphosate, and thus utility plants, e.g. Roundup soy will survive, while weeds will be destroyed. And the mechanism of harmfulness is that it is glyphosate, or this active substance, that, according to the World Health Organisation, is potentially carcinogenic. This is such a cautious term that this institution has formulated, but there are also American studies, very advanced on very large trials of fifty-six thousand, which indicate that exposure to glyphosate increases the incidence of e.g. for non-Hodgkin's lymphoma by more than 40%. Studies also cover other types of cancer. So it is this mechanism that causes us to do everything to, for example. the protein source used in feed, i.e. genetically modified soybean imported, may have been replaced by national protein sources which are not treated with Roundup spraying, the active substance glyphosate, and contain less of these carcinogens and therefore are simply healthier. So we have a health-promoting effect, and an effect supporting the development of Polish agriculture, because as we know, we spend about PLN 4 billion per year on imports of soy buckwheat. So if at least half of these imports were replaced by national sources of protein, about 2 billion could be left in the pockets of Polish farmers, maybe a little less, maybe more, depending on how much this can be replaced. I think it will work, because Minister Ardanowski is very involved in making this protein program start and in Polish feed there will be a protein that will grow in Polish farmers' fields. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u196	Mrs. Marshal. Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the draft law amending the Act on agri-food promotion funds, prints No 3455 and 3508. The first reading of the draft law took place at a committee meeting yesterday. On the basis of the Act of 22 May 2009, i.e. 10 years ago, nine funds have been created for the promotion of agri-food products: the promotion of milk, pork meat, beef, horse meat, sheep meat, grain and cereal products, fruit and vegetables, poultry meat and the promotion of fish. The main objective of the promotion funds is to promote marketing, increased consumption and promotion of agri-food products. The establishment of specific tasks carried out by promotion funds in a given year shall be the responsibility of representatives of industry organisations representing agricultural producers and processors operating in a given sector who form a separate management committee for each fund. The funding of promotion funds may be used to finance or co-finance promotion and information programmes, co-financing promotion and information actions eligible for EU support, financial support for measures to inform about the quality, characteristics and advantages of products covered by the Act, actions to promote the consumption of these products, thematic exhibitions and fairs, scientific research and development to improve the quality of products covered by the Act, market research on the consumption of products covered by the Act, producer training and processors, tasks relating to the participation of representatives of national industry organisations involved in the work of committees of various international organisations. The proposed amendment to the Act on Promotion Funds, which responds to the demands of oil plant representatives, envisages the creation of a new Fund for the Promotion of Oleist Plants, which would promote marketing, increased consumption and promotion of oil crops and their products, derived from the processing of rape, turnip rape, soya, flax and hemp, but also very important, bee products. Support from this bee product fund, which is a particular expression of understanding by the producers of rape for beekeepers, is worth emphasising and, of course, justified on the basis of bilateral relations, including the impact of bees' activity on the quality of the rape crop, which represents a 95% share in the national oil crop area. By pollinating the rape, bees increase the yield by 10%. The pollination of flowers by bees is beneficial not only to increase the yield, but also to the quality of the seeds. The Oleist Plant Promotion Fund will make payments of 0,2% net value of oil crops subject to tax on goods and services. It is estimated that the annual revenue on this fund will amount to nearly PLN 8 million. 15% appropriations are intended to be used to promote bee products. The promotion fund will only be managed by representatives of industry organisations representing agricultural producers and processors operating in the oil plant sector who will set up a separate management committee. These will include four representatives of oil plant producers, one representative of bee products producers, three representatives of processors and one representative of agricultural chambers. The members of the Management Board shall be notified and elected by representatives of the oil plant industry. The Managing Commission will decide on the financial allocation of the Oleist Plant Promotion Fund for specific tasks included in the annual financial plans. Promotion funds have played a positive role in the promotion of Polish food, contributing, of course, together with many other factors, among others. to increase the value of Polish food exports up to EUR 30 billion. The solutions proposed in the bill will further increase the food security of Poland, increase the incomes of farmers, increase the competitiveness of Polish agriculture and reduce the deficit of feed protein. The process of the draft law within the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development took place in an atmosphere of favor and acceptance, without a vote of objection. Therefore, on behalf of the committee, I request the support of the High House for the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

As I mentioned, Kukiz's parliamentary project15 emphasized the promotion of Polish agri-food products. The current law - and the revised proposal does not change this now - allows for support for products where the content of ingredients originating from outside Poland can be as high as 25%. This is an unacceptable solution because it leads to a situation where funds from Polish farmers' contributions support foreign farmers. We also drew attention to the need to focus the new fund's activities on foreign markets. It is abroad that there is the greatest potential for the sale of Polish products, which should be used. The Polish brand is still poorly recognizable outside our country's borders. The Fund would allocate 60% funds just for foreign stocks. And finally, I will remind you that the big one may be more and cheaper. One large fund could promote the Polish brand and proper eating habits more effectively than 10 fragmented ones. In conclusion, the proposed solutions in the draft amendment to the agri-food promotion fund law that is now under discussion should be assessed as needed, particularly in view of raising the awareness of poultry producers...
