ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u226	Mrs. Marshal! Honourable Members! Thank you very much for your appreciation for this project. I take these comments very seriously. The idea is not to create an unpractical existence, but to take such measures, such solutions that will effectively support the promotion of a certain part of our food production. This fund will be collected from farmers, and all promotion funds are funds that are owned by farmers, and I very much want to emphasise that. Processors are those who collect this penny and withdraw it to accounts once held at the Agricultural Market Agency and at the moment at the National Agricultural Support Centre, while these are farmers' funds. During an interesting discussion, which was on the committee, the voices of this discussion repeated... In general, the discussion concerned not only this particular fund, but the whole promotion fund law, whether it works well. I would like to say to Mr Pyzik that in February we prepared and submitted a very detailed report to the Committee on Agriculture on the effects of the promotion funds since 2009, it can be read. Since there have been and are still comments from some agricultural organisations primarily on the effectiveness of spending money from funds, on the sense and purpose of financing individual promotion programmes, I have decided that for the past two months, I do not remember exactly, full information on the expenditure of promotion funds is suspended on the website of the Ministry of Agriculture and it can be thoroughly verified which social organisation, what resources it had and what programme it was implementing. It seems to me that they most often criticize the promotion funds of those organizations which have made the protest their mark, but at the same time are unable to carry out effective promotional action themselves. I encourage all those who criticize the funds to apply for some promotional measures for consumers across the country, regions, areas where they have their structures, and show that they can use these funds better than those they criticize. There is a doubt whether to create a single fund, that is, Mr Sachajka's request from the Kukiz movement15. It seems to me that this would not be intentional because promotion funds come from farmers. Unfortunately, there is a dominant belief among farmers: my money is to go to a promotion that concerns my products. Whoever makes cow's milk isn't interested in selling horse meat. This is the first example on the shore. However, there is sector-specific thinking here and it would not necessarily be possible to manage this fund efficiently. The principle which was adopted in the 2009 Act - the law adopted or was initiated by Minister Sawicki, and its draft We have left him, he comes from the days of our first governments, the rule of law and justice, and I am grateful that he has undertaken work on this project - he assumes that the management of the fund is carried out collegially by those identified by the two environments directly affected by the fund. They are farmers - not farmers, from any organisation, but farmers representing producers - and the second group is processors who are interested in the promotional effect because they sell the final product. There are four to four, and one, the ninth person is a representative of the National Council of Agricultural Chambers, i.e. farmers have an advantage of five to four in fund management committees and can, even if there is no consensus, obtain through voting the implementation of such actions as are needed. The clarity of the spending of funds, which stems from the transparency of the information provided by the Ministry of Agriculture, the knowledge which persons, by name, are sitting on funds, on management committees, seems to be sufficient to be sure that it is well spent or not. I have no reason to question the direction of thinking or the principle of spending. Yes, we are also checking the state control services to verify that this money is not wasted, that it is spent in an appropriate, rational way. On the other hand, as far as the objectives are concerned, these committees define them by selecting from the catalogue those proposals, those projects which are submitted by social organisations. I don't think there's any better socialization. I very much respect the principle that the Ministry of Agriculture should try not to interfere, not to order, as Mrs Sunday suggested here, not to force the management committee to take any specific action. I believe that the social representatives, assuming that they were elected as representatives of interest groups, who are deeply involved in this, are best aware of what to spend on. I think that these funds are being spent in a reasonable way at the moment, but I'm sure there's still room for improvement. The big export effect of Poland did not come from anything either. It's also the work of people involved in promotion funds, it's the measures that also influence the recognition of Polish products in the world, it's the participation in all these exhibitions and trade fairs, etc. The promotion funds give us another very important advantage. These funds are envious of farmers in other countries. The funds provide an opportunity to allocate national funds to co-finance the acquisition of much more EU funding without breaking EU rules, in accordance with EU rules. If we did not have these funds, these private farmers' funds, we would not be able to co-finance the acquisition of funds from the European Union. Nor is it that farmers compete for the same customer. Well, there's a rule all over the world that you compete for the same customer, because if he eats more meat, then less bread, if he eats more pork meat, it's less poultry, less fish, it excludes each other in some way, it's obvious, but we try to reach these consumers through promotion. Promotion financed by Polish promotion funds is also the fight against imports, it is the convincing to buy Polish food, it is also supporting exports. These are the actions to which the funds go. I'm very happy because there's been a new phenomenon that I think very highly of. Namely, organisations dealing with different products are starting to cooperate with each other in the field of promotion. At the moment it is implemented by seven organizations dealing with soft fruit projects: Polish superfruit. They don't fight each other, they just agree that they promote fruit together, from strawberries to raspberries, blueberries, kamcha berries, arons... I can't remember what else is going on. They support each other, not fight each other. That also shows some new thinking. Ladies and gentlemen, this fund is intended to support the use of oil - oil from various oil plants. After all, Poland is not only a large producer of rape oil. The quality of cold-pressed oils is excellent, whether from linseed, or from flax, or from other plants. The promotion of oil, not just rape oil, is one of the main goals. Using this oil for both consumption purposes... Here we can still do a lot to make people believe that it is not waste, peanut oils or palm trees, somewhere from the countries of East Asia, only from Polish raw materials. These are very healthy oils to enrich our diet, with appropriate content and proportions of omega-3, omega-6 acids. I'm not a nutritionist, so I don't really know anything about it. This is one of the elements. The second element, for me, was extremely important, which was also underlined by the rapporteur, namely the promotion of rape buckwheat and buckwheat from other oil plants as an important component replacing modified American soya, post-extract buckwheat. It is the building of Polish agriculture, it is the provision of income to Polish farmers. This is an excellent role of these plants when it comes to crop rotation, improving soil fertility. It's also carbon absorption, it's carbon sequestration activities, that's pro-natural activities. The purpose of this Act is to confirm this effect during a transitional period in order to eliminate any doubts in this regard. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u238	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! If the United Kingdom ratifys this agreement, then it will take place in an agreed manner, in line with the agreement, which provides for a transitional period until the end of 2020. This law aims to regulate certain cases in such a way as to continue to treat Britain as a member of the EU until the end of the transitional period if the agreement is adopted. In particular, it is about British citizens staying permanently in Poland. They will be treated as citizens of other EU Member States. These British citizens in Poland have about 5700. There are also over 100 posted workers in Poland. It is also about Polish citizens who may be prosecuted by a European arrest warrant. As you know, Polish citizens do not seem to other countries unless appropriate agreements provide for this. And we have such an agreement within the EU, but when the United Kingdom comes out of the EU, then there will be some confusion as to whether or not, during the transition period, we will be able to issue our citizens chased by a European arrest warrant from the United Kingdom or not. For the sake of clarity, this law is about to regulate that by the end of 2020 the European arrest warrant will also apply to Polish citizens, because the United Kingdom will still be treated as a Member State in this period. There are a few exceptions to this treatment. They are provided for in the exit agreement and this law also applies to that agreement. Parliamentary Club Civic Platform - Civic Coalition will support the adoption of this bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u248	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present here today a draft law on the system of development institutions, which aims to regulate the rules and strengthen cooperation between the main institutions of the state which carry out its tasks for economic and social development. I am thinking primarily of the Polish Development Fund, the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, the Polish Agency for Enterprise Development, the Export Credit Insurance Corporation, the Polish Investment and Trade Agency and the Industrial Development Agency. These are the institutions of development. This project pursues a vision of their cooperation, coordination of their actions, which has been outlined in the framework of the Responsible Development Strategy. Previously, what was linked by these individual institutions was that they were all supervised by state entities. However, they were different entities, they were fixed, so to speak, in various ministries, they operate - that is, and now - in different forms: the bank, the agency, the company, they operate on the basis of their own separate regulations. An element of the vision outlined in the Responsible Development Strategy is, however, to seek synergies in this field or to fight for it, in order to strengthen this cooperation and, above all, to plan and coordinate it. In a nutshell This is the essence of the project I'm presenting here today. A few words may even be about the concept of an institution of development. It is not a Polish concept, nor is it a new concept. Next, I would like to talk a little bit about what it looks like in other countries. There is also a European aspect here, and these institutions also have a role to play in this field. Finally, I would like to briefly discuss some of the basic, already detailed solutions contained in the project. As far as development institutions, such systems, groups of capital, such institutions are concerned, it is worth pointing out that they operate primarily in Germany and France - they also operate in other countries, whereas these are so large European economies that we naturally look at them as a reference point. It should be noted that the German Kreditanstalt für Wiederaufbau plays a major role in the German economy and, if one can say so, sometimes complements certain market gaps. It happens that players participating on purely commercial terms in the market are not willing to make certain investments or carry out certain tasks, and that's where the player enters, for example. He participated in the privatisation of Deutsche Post or Deutsche Telekom. The main actions here are to support exports, to grant aid funds, above all to companies in the SME sector, and to support the development or expansion of the German economy outside the country. A similar concept, a little differently realized, is also in France. There we have, for example. The Deposit and Consignation Fund, which manages e.g. part of the pension schemes available on the French market. We also have such activities here in the area of investment, in the area of supporting the development of the country, in helping exports. These are models from developed, more developed economies than ours, where these institutions or groups of institutions are acting in a coordinated, orderly and, as I say, as if on the border, I would say whether taking into account the requirements of the country's development policy, but also respecting the principles of market playing. This idea, let's say, of this type of action, has guided us at the stage of creating a strategy for responsible development, which is also reflected in the presented project. The basic assumption of the project is that the various institutions that I listed, thanks to the merging role of the PFR, should focus precisely on one group, the Group of the Polish Development Fund. It is, of course, necessary to respect the separation of institutions. As I said, they have separate legal regimes, different specificities both in terms of, say, corporate and financial terms. Nevertheless, it is possible - this is what the draft law provides - to coordinate its own tasks. Yes, own tasks, these are still the own tasks of individual institutions. On the other hand, we want by adopting a document, which in this project is called the strategy of the Polish Development Fund Group, these institutions themselves impose a mechanism of coordination, harmonisation of their actions. It is about not having competitive action against each other, which has sometimes happened in the past. It is also about obtaining savings and achieving synergies between them. The mechanism proposed by the Act is, of course, merely a complement to existing coordination mechanisms, such as from the level of ownership supervision or from the level of cooperation between the various actors on the principle of freedom of contract. This type of cooperation and coordination is already taking place, but we are trying to give it a certain institutional framework in order to consolidate it, and so that certain mechanisms can be strengthened in law. It is also worth referring to more detailed solutions and to mention that the project lists the tasks carried out by the PFR. These tasks are to be carried out in accordance with the wording of the project on a market basis. They consist of both carrying out their own projects and carrying out joint projects within the PFR Group and coordinating all development institutions. In addition to carrying out business activities, the project also provides for the performance of tasks entrusted by government authorities, other bodies carrying out public tasks or local government units in the field of capital investment. We also have tasks carried out through a subsidiary of PFR - PFR Ventures. These include, in particular, financing for entrepreneurs, in particular for SMEs, in areas such as capital investment, support for entrepreneurs, environmental protection, research, development and innovation, training and advice, recruitment and recruitment of disadvantaged workers, disabled workers, repair of damage caused by certain natural disasters, social projects for transport for residents of remote regions, infrastructure, investments in broadband infrastructure, preservation of cultural heritage, multi-purpose sport infrastructure, recreational infrastructure, local infrastructure. The financing conditions will, of course, be compatible with the common market within the meaning of Article. 107 Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. The provision creates a legal framework for the involvement of public funds in the financing of these PFR tasks. They will come, i.e., they may come, because they are a legal framework, from the budget of the European Union, from special subsidies from the state budget, and at the justified request of the fund from the increase in the capital of the PFR through the transfer by the Minister of Treasury Finance of securities for this purpose. In the latter case, this will also apply to the tasks of the fund carried out on a commercial basis, but within the scope of the catalogue of objectives indicated in the project, which I mentioned earlier. PFR commitments for borrowing or issuing bonds will be guaranteed by the Treasury on behalf of which the Council of Ministers will act at the request of the Minister of Finance in this case. In conclusion, the draft law is an important step towards the creation and further development of an integrated system of development institutions called the PFR Group. This concept is about coordinating, harmonising the activities of institutions that already exist. They exist, act and carry out their tasks, but the idea is to make them work more coherently. They shall carry out their tasks in accordance, taking into account the economic policy of the Council of Ministers, with the division of competences and with integrated supervision. Development institutions will be required to adopt a common strategy to diagnose the market situation and to identify ways of coordinating tasks in the field of support or services provided to businesses. The obligation of cooperation between development institutions will be introduced to implement a common strategy on the principles of coordination and mutual complementing competences, which in the hope of the projectors will allow better use of resources and competences, generate savings, synergies and the vision of responsible development strategy, which is sometimes summarised by the English expression "one-stop shop." We want institutions to work together in this area, and entrepreneurs who seek help know where to go. Later, perhaps within the group and certain specializations may be directed e.g. to PAIH, if they are interested in exporting or to PARP, if they are interested in EU funds. This is the fundamental objective of the project and I would therefore like to ask you kindly to accept it. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u249	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first vote will be taken by Mr Andrzej Noblet, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u250	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the PiS Parliamentary Club on the government draft law on the system of development institutions, print No 3479. The aim of this draft law is to regulate the rules and strengthen cooperation between the main institutions of the State implementing economic and social development measures using economic instruments. In order to achieve this objective, mechanisms will be introduced to ensure coordination and mutual complementarity of the following entities: Polish Development Fund SA, Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, Polish Agency for Enterprise Development, Export Credit Insurance Corporation, Polish Investment and Trade Agency SA and Industrial Development Agency SA. In the current legal situation, these entities carry out their tasks separately. Their legal form is not the same, because we have stock companies, a state bank and a state legal person. The only common feature of these institutions is their subordinateness to the authorities of the State. It is therefore appropriate to adopt legal solutions that will allow for a rapid synergy effect. This concept is adopted in many EU countries, e.g. in the UK, France, Germany, Italy, Spain, Hungary and Slovakia. An important feature of these systems is their consolidation within the dominant entity, as well as the clearly defined scope of the tasks pursued and the coordination of activities in areas defined as development. It is also worth noting that the development institutions also play a key role in the implementation of the Investment Plan for Europe, adopted by the European Commission in 2014. In the European Commission's view, market failures can lead to a reduction in investment and thus to lower economic development in the future than that which would be economically effective. In addition, development investments allow for greater synergies between EU funds, funding provided by the European Investment Bank and Member States' national programmes. The strategy for responsible development adopted by the Government of PiS assumes the regulation of the functioning of development institutions that serve the implementation of Poland's development policy, including by supplementing the private sector. The proposed law will also allow for the expansion of financial instruments offered by state development institutions. Such actions are necessary to strengthen companies operating in Poland. Combining the potential of all development institutions will achieve synergies and increase opportunities for obtaining market funding, facilitating particularly large projects. The above-mentioned strategy for responsible development provides that the institution integrating the system of development institutions will be the Polish Development Fund SA and the other development institutions will cooperate within the Polish Development Fund Group. The permanent mechanism of cooperation between development institutions is to be ensured by the Council of the Polish Development Fund Group. The Council will act on the principles of consensus and equality between all institutions. The Act provides that the Polish Development Fund will finance entrepreneurs, especially micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the bill now being presented during further legislative work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u280	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Today, a meeting of the Energy and Treasury Commission took place in which the first reading of the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on the amendment of the excise duty law and certain other laws, the Energy Efficiency Act and the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels took place. First of all, I would like to point out that the proposed amendments to the laws mentioned by me are highly pro-social. The first one allows to continue in the second half of 2019 to protect electricity consumers in Poland from energy price increases. The second is to reach out to those who have invested in energy efficiency, hoping for state support, but have not yet received this support for various reasons. The third amendment will allow for the request for support for the purchase of electric cars by individuals, i.e. so-called. ordinary citizens. Now I would like to move on to a detailed discussion of the provisions of the bill and the amendments made to the committee. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 It is proposed to amend the Law of 28 December 2018 amending the Law on excise duties, i.e. so-called. Act on electricity prices. The amendment corrects the functioning mechanism to stabilise electricity prices, clarifying doubts as to the compatibility of the law with European law. As a result, the project introduces a differentiation of the mechanism for the first and second half of 2019. In the first half of 2019 all end-users of electricity will not pay higher prices than in 2018. In the second half of 2019 as many as 98% of final customers, i.e. over 17 million consumers, will not feel an increase in electricity prices. These recipients will be among others.: households with around 15 million micro-enterprises, which are about 2 million, small businesses with about 57,000, hospitals and public finance units, including local governments. Average and large entrepreneurs in the second half of the year will be covered by a 95% reduction in the transitional fee as well as other recipients. and a reduction in excise duty. In addition, the proposed rules provide for the possibility for medium-sized and large enterprises to apply for funding charged to electricity consumed in the second half of 2019 under the de minimis aid scheme. Two amendments to the Rule were tabled during the committee's work. 1 a project aimed at linking the deadline for amending the agreements concluded between trading companies and final recipients with the deadline for issuing the Regulation by the Minister for Energy. Such a link exists already in the current provisions of the Law of 28 December 2018 and there is no need for additional deadlines for amending these agreements. As a result of the amendments made to the Article. In point 2, point 1 is deleted. a and the wording added in Article has been amended. Article 6 (6) (a) 5. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 A revision of the Energy Efficiency Act is proposed. The amendment involves extending the validity of energy efficiency certificates. This is so important that some of them were not even issued by the Energy Regulatory Office, so the entities to which they belong did not have the possibility to sell these certificates on the stock exchange. Some energyists point out, perhaps rightly so, that energy efficiency is the first fuel that should be used in the energy mix. So we propose not to leave with such a big problem the players who have invested in this efficiency, because in the future they may not make any further decisions in this regard. During the work of the committee, amendments were made to the Article. 2 by deleting in Article. Points 1 and 2 to avoid doubts as to the compatibility of the provisions of the draft with the Energy Efficiency Directive, thus giving the article a new wording. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels is amended. This amendment is intended to enable the acquisition of electric vehicles and hydrogen-using vehicles by natural persons not engaged in economic activities to be requested. Art. 4 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing In turn, the article. 6 introduces an obligation for the Minister of Energy to issue a regulation to the Act on electricity prices within 21 days of the date of entry into force of the Act, so that the entire payment procedure for the fund for energy companies will be initiated and contracts with final recipients will be amended, which have not yet been amended. We propose that the law enter into force on the day following the date of publication. The entry into force of the Act on the day following the date of its publication shall be justified by the division of 2019 into two mechanisms to stabilise electricity prices for final customers. The exception is Art. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act as amended by Art. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. This deadline is due to the need to maintain the validity of energy efficiency certificates. Maintaining low electricity prices, protecting Poles from rising electricity prices is a process. For this reason, we present this law today, so that in the second half of the year Polish electricity consumers will also be effectively protected and not feel, looking at their bills, an increase in electricity prices. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u322	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Government of the Republic of Poland, I am pleased to present today a draft law amending the Act on greenhouse gas emissions trading and certain other laws. The drafting and submission of the bill, which we will be dealing with today, is due to the need to implement into the Polish legal order the provisions of the European Parliament and Council Directive of 14 December 2016 on the reduction of national emissions of certain atmospheric pollutants. We're talking about the so-called. NEC Directive. The NEC Directive has established obligations for Member States to reduce the emissions of anthropogenic pollutants into the atmosphere of the following compounds: sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides, non-methane volatile organic compounds, ammonia and fine dust, PM 2,5, and requires the preparation, adoption and implementation of national air pollution reduction plans, as well as monitoring and reporting of emissions to air. The proposed changes aim to enable the process of collecting data from industrial and energy companies which are covered by the greenhouse gas emissions trading scheme, i.e. so-called. the ETS, where this is done for the purpose of determining emission allowances during the next trading period and this will take place under new rules in line with those introduced by the Directive of 14 March 2018. Until 30 September this year. Poland shall forward to the European Commission the list of installations together with the necessary data to determine the allocation of emission allowances for the reference period beginning in 2021. Solutions were also envisaged to bring Polish legislation on the greenhouse gas emissions trading system into line with changing socio-economic conditions. As you know, the previous novel of this law, this directive took place four years ago. After 4 years, a number of things have changed and changes are therefore needed to make the law more consistent with economic, administrative and legal realities. I will briefly allow myself to present to the High House the most important demands of the law we are discussing today. Firstly, the proposal introduces a number of provisions aimed at giving investors greater freedom to make strategic decisions. The proposed changes relate to the accounting of investment costs of the specific tasks included in the national investment plan. In particular, they rely on the possibility of balancing the allowances issued for other investment sentences, in the event that the investor ceases to carry out a task or does not achieve approved compliance indicators, i.e. parameters to demonstrate that there has been a reduction in emissions. In the event of an application for balancing the emission allowances issued, it has been decided to suspend the recovery procedure. Once the Minister for the Environment has given his consent, the procedure will be terminated as unobtrusive, allowing the ETS entities to retain the public aid received in the form of emission allowances issued. The draft introduces new rules for the fixing of emission allowances during the trading period beginning in 2021. The amendments made mainly to establish new procedural requirements for the allocation of emission allowances are intended to implement the provisions of Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2019/331. One of the requirements introduced by this Regulation is the obligation for operators to draw up so-called installations. monitoring methodology plans to ensure that operators provide detailed and high quality information and data on installation characteristics and sub-installations, production processes that will be collected on the basis of a detailed description of the monitoring methodology and data sources and reflect actual information on the operation of the installation and its processes. The monitoring methodology plans will be approved by the competent authorities to authorise greenhouse gas emissions after obtaining the opinion of the National Centre for Balancing and Emissions Management, or KOBIZE. In addition, procedures for examining applications for emission allowances from the reserve have been simplified in order to reduce the administrative burden on the operator and to reduce the duration of the procedure. The legislator has provided for a procedure in which KOBIZE will contact the operators directly to address the deficiencies and errors identified in the applications, but it will be the Minister responsible for environmental matters who will be informed at each stage of the deficiencies identified in the application and of the extent of the necessary additions and amendments. The draft law imposes an additional task on KOBIZ to develop periodic forecasts of changes in activity for individual sectors of the economy, and the competent minister will establish assumptions for such a forecast for the sector concerned and provide the relevant information and data to KOBIZ. As regards the transposition of the NEC Directive, it should be noted that Poland's emission reduction commitments relate to two periods, which cover the years 2020 to 2029 and from 2030. These commitments have been defined respectively for both periods mentioned above, inter alia. for SO2 - by 59 and 70% , for NOx - by 30 and 39% That's right. The main tool to help achieve the indicated reductions in emissions of five pollutants is the National Air Pollution Reduction Programme, for which the basis is introduced precisely in the draft submitted by the High Commission. It is proposed that the national programme be adopted by the Council of Ministers in the form of a resolution at the request of the Minister responsible for Environment. The national programme is to be updated at least every 4 years or within 18 months of the transmission of the latest national emission balance or emission forecasts if, in accordance with the reported data, national emission reduction commitments are not met or are at risk of non-compliance. The draft law also identifies the correct emission reduction path, which is designated as a linear reduction in emissions of five substances from 2020 to 2030. Where the level of reduction of emissions of substances adopted in the national programme is not achieved or there is a risk that it will not be achieved, the Minister for the Environment is to inform the relevant ministers of the existence of such risks and competent ministers will then be required to develop proposals for additional measures and measures. In order to properly monitor the implementation of the actions identified in the national programme, competent ministers will be required to draw up and communicate to the Environment Minister information on the actions and measures contained in the national programme, and we assume that this information should be submitted by 31 January each year starting in 2020. A very important aspect of the regulated subject is agriculture, which is to a large extent responsible for emissions of ammonia into the atmosphere. It is worth remembering here that about 94% ammonia emissions come from agricultural activities. In order to reduce these emissions, new obligations and sanctions for non-compliance with them are introduced in the Fertilisation and Fertilisation Act. Therefore, the law also prohibits the use of ammonium carbonate fertilisers. A legislative delegation is also introduced for the Minister responsible for Agriculture to develop a code of good agricultural practice for reducing ammonia emissions. The Code will indicate best practices for nitrogen management, use, storage of fertilisers and animal housing rules that reduce ammonia emissions. The NEC Directive also introduced an obligation to monitor the impact of air pollution on ecosystems and to prepare reports to the European Commission and the European Environment Agency in this area. We therefore proposed to amend the Environmental Inspection Act accordingly. We predict that the bill will enter into force 14 days from the date of its announcement. This is an important act because its adoption will stabilise the legal situation of traders covered by the trading system and will create a legal basis for carrying out the tasks resulting from the law in the fourth subsequent trading period. The transposition of the NEC Directive will significantly improve air quality in Poland. Therefore, I ask you, High Izbo, to consider this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u323	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Czesław Sobierajski, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u324	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! One of the main elements of the bill is the transposition of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 December 2016 - the Minister spoke about it in detail - on the reduction of national emissions of certain atmospheric pollutants in which obligations for Poland to reduce emissions were laid down. They relate to periods that cover the years from 2020 and 2030. The Minister mentioned it, and he deepened it, he said exactly what it was about. The draft law introduces provisions to help Poland reach the emission reduction levels of the five pollutants set out in the Directive and thus allow for significant improvements in air quality and reduce the impact of air pollution on the health of residents. The second objective of the draft law is to implement in part the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 March 2018. The transposition of the Directive to the extent provided for by the Act aims to safeguard the most urgent regulatory needs due to the need to carry out the data collection process for the purpose of determining the range of emission allowances in the subsequent trading period 2021 and 2030, under the new rules introduced by Directive 2018/410. The project introduces changes to the calculation of investment costs of the specific tasks included in the national investment plan. A positive change will be the possibility of balancing issued emission allowances for other investment tasks in the event of failure to carry out any task or of the failure of the investor to achieve approved compliance indicators. Facilitates have been put in place for traders to provide explanations and to correct the application for allocation of emission allowances from the allowance reserve. In addition, the deadline for giving an opinion on the completeness of the data contained in the application and the correctness of the calculations carried out has been adapted to the reality for KOBIZE. Changes to the environmental fee scheme have been proposed: the amount of the allocation fee will continue to depend on the number of emission allowances concerned and the unit fee will have to be adopted for the year in which the allowances were issued and not for the year in which they were allocated. It's important. The project also provides for more responsibilities for the KoBIZ due to the increased importance of EU climate policy and air protection policy. His new task will be among others. monitoring the tasks in the area of air protection policy, which the Minister mentioned more widely, as well as the preparation of analyses, reviews and assessments of its functioning. Thanks to these solutions, the environment resort will be supported in the implementation of many air protection projects. In summary, this law is intended to implement the directive and at the same time to ensure that we live in better air and that businesses operate less, less burden. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u338	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for speaking on behalf of the clubs and asking questions. The Court rejected our proposal on 19 March 2019 and there is therefore no question of continuing to discuss whether or not to implement it, but how we are to implement it. It should be noted that we are in a legal situation that is subject to change also on the part of the European Commission. Delegated Regulations, which defined how implementation would take place, were published after the formal deadline by which Member States were obliged to transpose the Directive. Since the Delegated Regulation on Statistics appeared in November and the subsequent regulation on how this Directive was implemented only in December 2018, Mrs, we have therefore received additional information both in November and in December, as well as in March on the way and extent of transposition of this Directive into the Polish legal system. Thanks to this, we are working as soon as possible today on the draft law that implements this directive. As regards the work of the Ministry of the Environment and my services, Poland, in accordance with the provisions of this directive, although we have not yet been subject to the law transposing the directive, has already prepared a It was transmitted to the European Commission in April 2019 in accordance with the objectives, in accordance with the provisions of the Directive itself. It's not here. there is also no statement of any formal shortcomings on the part of Poland, which Mrs Lenartowicz was gracious to ask. We have had consultations resulting from administrative work and the exchange of information with the European Commission as part of the EU Pilot query. This, on the other hand, is in no way equivalent to a formal statement of non-implementation. As regards Mr Zbigniew Ajchler's question on the concrete solutions put in place for agriculture, I would like to remind you that this document is being constructed under the auspices of the Minister for the Environment, while sector-specific contributions are prepared by the relevant ministers. In this case, the minister responsible is the Minister of Agriculture, who proposed that this action, of which you were graciously speaking, should take place in a perspective of not 4 but 12 hours. We have a longer period there and it is good practice, not applicable law. So we have here at this stage the opportunity to act on behalf of farmers. As far as implementation costs are concerned, at this stage we predicted the amount of PLN 8 million for the sectoral programmes in question, which are to be prepared by KOBIZE on the basis of contributions from individual ministries. There are no additional charges, there are no administrative burdens that this law would generate. So much for my answers. Thank you very much, Marshal. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u340	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the law on the monitoring system for road and rail freight transport and certain other laws. This project introduces comprehensive legal regulations for the marketing of fuel oil and diesel fuel for fuel purposes and other liquid fuel fuels. The primary objective of the proposed regulation will be to reduce the administrative burden on operators of these excise products so far. Currently, operators selling fuel oil are required to collect paper declarations from the purchaser on the use of this oil for fuel purposes and then to draw up and forward monthly statements to the competent head of the tax office. According to the data available in 2018, this was the case for over 1200 entrepreneurs. These entities also collected almost 1.1 million paper statements in 2017 and 2018. In the peak months of the heating period, these operators receive 70 000. statements. The original statements should be kept by the trader for a period of 5 years from the end of the calendar year in which they were drawn up and made available for inspection. The determination of whether the fuel oil has been used for its intended purpose requires an analysis of the statements on the use of these products and the carrying out of tax checks or ex post customs-subsistence checks. These solutions were mainly based on paper documents, which is not in line with the expectations of controlled entrepreneurs. The process of both collecting and storing paper statements is time-consuming, cumbersome and, as experience points out, also generates many other problems. It follows from the data held for the years 2012 and 2014 that around 100,000 were verified annually in the framework of tax controls and tax audits. statements, of which more than 30% had formal errors. This, in turn, gave rise to the obligation to carry out further control activities, involving forces both on the part of the entrepreneur and the authorities. Replacing paper declarations by electronic declarations, as foreseen by the draft law, will lead to the elimination of the burdensome administrative obligations currently imposed on businesses by the collection and storage of paper declarations on the use of fuel fuels. On the administrative side, however, it will eliminate time- and labour-intensive verification of such documents and allow for a more efficient use of the personnel available. This law computerizes the process of submitting and storing statements about the use of fuel fuels, which is another step in building digital Poland and moving away from traditional formalities, i.e. paper. This is due to the fact that the Act assumes a fuller use of information systems already available to the National Tax Administration. The solutions contained in the proposal presented in the High House will also help to seal the tax system for the marketing of these products. The draft law constitutes a further step in the process of sealing the tax system, as it eliminates the existing risk of irregularities in the fuel sector which benefit from a reduced rate of excise duty and, having characteristics similar to or identical to those of motor fuels, were used for propulsion purposes and therefore not intended to be used. There is a risk of an increase in the trend towards irregularities in the fuel sector. This brings concrete losses to the state budget. Exposed in 2012 and 2014 losses only in excise duties amounted to about PLN 400 million. At this point, I would like to point out that the use of fuel fuels for propulsion purposes has not been entirely inhibited. Last month, the National Tax Administration, in cooperation with the Central Police Investigation Office and the Public Prosecutor's Office, carried out a spectacular action on irregularities in the circulation of fuel oil. The Polish-German criminal group brought fuel oil from Germany to Poland to sell it as motor fuel. As it turns out, the organisation of 6 000 has been abandoned since 2014. illegal transport. In this case only the losses of the State Treasury are estimated at over PLN 100 million. It is therefore also justified to take legislative action, which is reflected in the draft law, in the context of the protection of the vital interests of the State Treasury. At the same time, it is worth noting that, for the purposes of sealing the turnover of fuel fuels, the Act uses existing mechanisms, i.e. the arrangements contained in the Law on the monitoring system for road and rail freight transport, consisting of monitoring under the electronic system of carriage of goods, with which there is a risk of fraud. Given that these solutions have in practice brought tangible benefits, the proposed Act also decided to include the monitoring system in its entirety in fuel oil trade, with the exception of the retail trade in fuel oils. This exception concerns sales at points of trade, e.g. construction centres, fuel for oil furnaces offered in packaging prepared for retail sale. Such sales will be linked to the obligation to record them in the relevant register, the copy of which will be sent electronically to the competent tax office. The project assumes that all participants in the fuel market will be required to register once for this purpose. This also applies to individuals who heat their homes with this kind of energy medium. In order to simplify registration, it is possible to perform this activity from behind the desk. Anyone who has access to the computer and the Internet will be able to register using the electronic form provided. For this purpose, from 14 August 2019 onwards, this form will be made available on two portals: the PUESC portal and the tax portal. The entire registration operation without leaving the house should take no more than a few minutes. In addition, a transitional period is foreseen for all until 31 March 2020 in which paper declarations on the use of fuel fuels may be made, provided that the oil user fails to register for various reasons. The registration of all operators in the fuel market is necessary to ensure the integrity of the system and the safety of trade in those products. The possibility for tax authorities to verify the data contained in simplified registrations will reduce the risk on the part of the operator supplying fuel. The supply of fuel to all customers will be reported to the transport and marketing monitoring system. Such a solution will seal the circle of sellers and buyers of fuel fuels, and the use of an electronic transport monitoring system will allow for the ongoing analysis of supply chain and fuel fuel consumption sites. Thanks to existing solutions it will be possible to monitor in real time the route of transport and react quickly, e.g. in cases where the goods are delivered to a place other than that resulting from the declaration of carriage. Thus, the risk of irregularities will be virtually eliminated and the fuel fuel trading system will actually be sealed. The use of IT tools will improve the surveillance process and control of the correct use of fuel fuels. The activities of the National Tax Administration bodies will be able to be carried out using electronic systems, while limiting reporting by entrepreneurs. The analytical capacity of national tax authorities will be increased, e.g. as regards the determination of the actual consumption of fuel oil purchased at a certain reduced rate of excise duty. At present, as has been said, this is very inefficient on the part of the national tax authorities, and because of the documentation of the use of oil in paper form, it also overly engages entrepreneurs, which is an undesirable phenomenon. High Izbo! Bottom line. The proposed bill comprehensively regulates the trading of fuel fuels, including the obligations of the participants in this trading. It introduces solutions based on an IT environment, meeting entrepreneurs' expectations and implementing them by reducing the current administrative burden. This project should also be seen as a continuation of the measures sealing the tax system in Poland, which will ultimately protect businesses from unfair competition and, by means of the proposed regulations, will in fact shift the burden of verifying the customers of fuel fuels to the National Tax Administration. Thank you very much.

Those receiving fuel oil and those selling in the course of their business, in addition to the suspension of excise duty on fuel oils, will be subject to registration. The introduction of this obligation is also necessary to ensure the leakage of fuel oil. The use of IT solutions for monitoring the circulation of fuel oils must lead to a reduction of existing and burdensome administrative obligations for entrepreneurs, as already mentioned by my esteemed previous speaker. The activities of the control authorities will be able to be carried out using electronic systems, while reducing the reporting by entrepreneurs. This also increases the analytical capacity of national tax authorities. Given that the overarching objective of the regulation is to protect the financial interests of our country, they are justified and obligations imposed on those involved in this process should be considered proportionate. The project will in no way affect the economic and social situation of Poles. The revision will have little effect on increasing the administrative burden associated with the one-off registration obligation. The draft law shall not be submitted to the competent authorities and institutions of the European Union. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u344	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present an opinion on the draft law amending the law on the monitoring system for road and rail freight transport and certain other laws, print No 3474. As we read in the explanatory memorandum, the basic purpose of the proposed law is to seal the tax system for fuel oil and diesel for fuel and other liquid fuel fuels which, due to their use for fuel purposes, have been subject to a reduced excise duty. An important objective of the Act is also to reduce the administrative burden for oil traders by eliminating the obligation to obtain paper statements from purchasers and store them for a period of 5 years for the purpose of verifying compliance with the conditions for applying a reduced rate for these products. The paper statements will therefore be replaced by an electronic registration system for recipients and an electronic confirmation of receipt and destination for fuel purposes, as the Minister has said in detail. The turnover of fuel oils, including those carried out without physical displacement, will be monitored by an electronic system processing data on the transport of goods and the trading of fuel oils as referred to in the Transport Monitoring System Act. Operators using fuel oils, including those physically not engaged in business activities, will be required to register as an oil-using operator, indicating the location of the heating equipment and subsequently confirming receipt of delivered fuel oils, which will replace the previous paper confirmation. Operators using fuel oil and carrying out sales in the course of their business activities outside the procedure of suspension of excise duty on fuel oils will be subject to registration. With regard to the placing on the market of fuel oils, the transport and marketing monitoring system with means of transport equipped with a GPS system which enables the monitoring of the route, indicating the place of delivery, eliminates the risk of irregularities, including in particular the delivery of the goods to another place. The use of IT tools is intended to facilitate the day-to-day control of the turnover of fuel oil subject to reduced rates, reducing the scale of irregularities, and to reduce the number of disputes arising between traders and tax authorities against illegible or incorrect paper declarations. The advantages of these solutions are visible. New obligations for both registration and acknowledgement of receipt may be difficult for those physically not engaged in business. Any facilitations addressed to them will result in burdens for entrepreneurs. There is a threat to the safety of existing customers who, for various reasons, will not be able to register, because it will not be possible to supply oil at a reduced rate for operators not registered in the system. The question arises as to whether the inclusion of specific obligations also for small-scale trading remains proportionate. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Why do we today come to the High House, to the ladies and gentlemen of the House with this bill? This is because the VAT sealing system has changed the Modus operandi. In the past, the problem of fuel oils, which were converted into diesel, was quite a serious problem. However, later organised crime changed to simpler forms of crime, which resulted in greater profitability, such as fuel, petrol, diesel, which were imported under intra-Community acquisitions. We have studied this for a very long time, working primarily with entrepreneurs, both small and large, with the two largest associations. One fuel association brings together large distributors, the other small ones. We've had conversations for a very long time. This is the result of these discussions. In fact, there is no problem with deliveries for individuals, as you mentioned, who want to existentially purchase fuel oil, because it is a carrier, firstly, very expensive, and secondly, it is not worth transporting this carrier in small packages. In fact, much larger quantities are profitable. 30 kg is for use, so to speak, more recreational. It's about some fireplaces, some kind of device. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u366	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Falling into a difficult or irrecoverable debt should not push anyone to the social margin, should not take away everything overnight. Therefore, the Ministry of Justice has prepared groundbreaking solutions to secure the exit from debt and restore normal life without ever fearing the bailiff. Today, Poles who overestimated their financial opportunities will lose their jobs, which will be affected by illness or an unfortunate random event and who are unable to pay their debts, are most often subject to chamber executions. They lose their house, their apartment, practically all their wages. The more they earn, the more the bailiff takes away from them, and the debt that grows over the years is still impossible to repay. As a result of this situation, many people are being pushed into the grey zone. They are exposed to depression or family problems. Creditors can't recover their debts anyway, because the last proverbial shirt has already been taken off the debtors. The scale of the problem is shown by numbers: it is estimated that more than 2 million Poles cannot cope with overdue debts, which totals almost PLN 50 billion. The mechanism that secures this situation is consumer bankruptcy. This mechanism benefits both the debtor and the creditor. Thanks to it you can comprehensively solve the debt problem, get a chance to start again and, as far as possible, repay creditors. At present, however, the courts reject almost half of applications for consumer bankruptcy. It is estimated that 6552 consumer bankruptcy was declared in 2018, with more than 12 000 requests for such bankruptcy being filed. We see progress because in 2014 only 31 bankruptcies were recorded with 300 applications. The first change is that it is sufficient to declare bankruptcy that the debtor is insolvent without examining the reasons for insolvency when opening insolvency proceedings. This is a very important change because it takes into account any type of debt that is often caused by recklessness, overestimation of its financial capacity, illness or other foreseeable circumstances that may result in a loss of liquidity. The reasons for insolvency will, of course, be taken into account, but only at the stage of the repayment plan. We assume two repayment plans: a standard up to 3 years and up to 7 years in the event of a reckless debt being incurred, the debtor was somehow guilty. Of course, we rule out all cases of gross negligence or situations where someone deliberately incurs debts so as not to pay them back later, and when someone commits fraud. Such extreme situations will be eliminated from this system. We introduce successful solutions e.g. in the UK, where almost 100,000 are announced. consumer bankruptcy per year. There are about 130 thousand people in the similar population in Canada. Bankruptcy proceedings per year, in Germany about 80,000, while in France almost 200,000 cases are conducted annually. This shows that the scale of using this type of mechanism in Western Europe is incomparably greater than what we see in our country. Hence, the solutions we are proposing are based on the observation of the experience of other countries. The second very important change is the retribution of this procedure. Today, these proceedings last an average of 2 years. Consumers often complain that proceedings are conducted on a chronic basis. The average duration of proceedings is 2 years and almost 40% of cases are cases lasting more than 6 months. By handing insolvency proceedings over to the restructuring advisors, we want to prevent this. The court will decide to open insolvency proceedings and then refer the case to the judge, not the Commissioner, but the restructuring advisor. The court will only intervene in the case of a complaint. I would also like to draw attention to a very important point, namely the possibility of speeding up the procedure by the fact that proceedings lasting more than 6 months will be included in the repayment plan period. The repayment plan for proceedings lasting more than 6 months will therefore be shorter. This is another very important guarantee for people seeking consumer bankruptcy. It will simply make these actions quicker. The third very important change we are proposing is to introduce the possibility of an agreement before bankruptcy. In the UK there are about 50,000. That kind of deal at 11,000. classic consumer bankruptcy. The solution is of great interest, especially among people from the so-called. the middle class whose earnings allow them to reach an agreement with creditors without costly and long-term insolvency proceedings. This mechanism is also envisaged. For those in a very difficult life situation, affected by chronic illness, we provide for termination of proceedings. In turn for people who are able to get straight, but not at the moment - it can be e.g. young people after graduating, who recklessly borrowed a few loans, but may be able to repay them in the future - we predict so-called. conditional redemption lasting 5 years. There is a period that allows the debtor to find a job, get a job opportunity. That way he can start a repayment plan. So that the possibility for debtors to enter into agreements with creditors, the failure of the court to examine the debtor's culpability when opening insolvency proceedings, the removal from the court's administration of the simplest cases of consumer bankruptcy, which will significantly speed up proceedings, the remission of liabilities without a repayment plan, the conditional remission - are solutions that will certainly help to improve the situation of debtors, people who have often not fallen into debt spirals and then are sentenced to the grey zone. We want these opportunities to be available to a larger group of citizens. It is estimated that there may be up to 30,000 a year. bankruptcy proceedings. It is possible to judge, possible to settle in mode, through the so-called. restructuring advisors. I would like to remind you that the bill has already been passed. There is a solution providing for supervision by the Minister of Justice of the restructuring work which complements this system. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I remain convinced that the proposed legal arrangements will make it possible to significantly improve insolvency procedures, long-term procedures, which should allow a faster start for those who are trapped in debt often without their fault and fear entering a cumbersome, unfriendly procedure for the defendant while maintaining the legitimate interests of creditors. I ask the High House to support this project. The government is hoping for a quick process. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u367	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u368	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition club on the government draft law amending bankruptcy law and some other laws, print No 3480. The Government's draft law amending the bankruptcy law and certain other laws aims at simplifying the insolvency procedure for consumer insolvency and thus increasing the number of insolvency proceedings. The law on consumer bankruptcy introduced by the Government by the Civic Platform - PSL on 1 January 2015 significantly changed the legal situation of insolvent natural persons not engaged in business activities. New, better conditions have been created for declaring consumer bankruptcy, resulting in a rapid increase in the number of applications in this case. In 2015, 5616 applications for consumer bankruptcy were received by the courts, which represented almost a 20-fold increase compared to 2014. When a year later, i.e. On 1 January 2016, the subject of consumer bankruptcy was significantly extended to former entrepreneurs during the year since the removal from the relevant register or cessation of business activity, the increase in the impact of applications was almost avalanche. As a result of this regulation, there has been a considerable burden on the courts and an extension of the time taken to deal with cases. In the course of the bankruptcy procedure against former entrepreneurs, it is necessary for the court to examine the obligations arising from the Article. 491 Bankruptcy rights, establishing lists of claims, resolving problems with bankruptcy and other matters directly affecting the extension of proceedings. This proposal proposes a number of simplifications in the procedure, including, inter alia,: introducing the possibility of declaring bankruptcy of a non-economic person in a simplified procedure without the appointment of a judge of the Commissioner, introducing the possibility for debtors to enter into agreements with creditors with a limited participation of courts under the direction of a licensed restructuring adviser, giving up the need for a court to examine at the stage of declaring the insolvency of the debtor in order to bring or deepen the debt situation, allowing the debtor not to sell quickly all the assets subject to execution in a prepared liquidation procedure, which will increase the flexibility available to the debtor of the option of rapid exit from insolvency, reporting claims directly to the syndicated restructuring adviser, and not, as is currently the case, to the court, and establishing that the waiver of liabilities without a repayment plan will be conditional, which is intended to prevent too rash remission of liabilities. These are the basic directions for changes to the bill. The draft law also amends six other laws related to the consumer bankruptcy procedure. To sum up, the proposed solutions aim to simplify the consumer insolvency procedure, which is to increase the number of cases under consideration and facilitate the situation of debtors who are in very difficult situations. However, the bill requires a broad analysis and discussion. Therefore, the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Club is in favour of sending the project to further legislative work. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u389	The Marshal of the Sejm, after consulting the Bureau of the Sejm, proposes that the Sejm refer the government draft law amending the bankruptcy law and certain other laws contained in print No 3480 to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. We proceed with the examination of item 16 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on auditors, audit firms and public oversight and some other laws (print No 3481). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Leszek Skiba to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u390	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a bill amending the statutory auditor's bill, print No 3481. The draft law aims to amend the Act on statutory auditors, audit firms and public oversight in order to strengthen public oversight of the activities of statutory auditors and audit firms. Experience so far has shown that there is a need for greater control of the activities of audit firms, including services other than statutory audit of financial statements, including in particular those related to the functioning of the capital market. Currently, the public oversight body, i.e. The Audit Supervisory Commission does not have sufficient tools to control the quality of the services provided by audit firms in this respect, as the public oversight system is largely based on indirect supervision. A significant part of the public oversight tasks has been delegated to the Polish Chamber of Auditors, which carries out checks on the quality of statutory audits of entities other than bodies of public interest and carries out investigations and sanctions against them. Difficulties in the implementation of public oversight tasks by the organisation of the local government, in particular as regards the carrying out of audits, have highlighted the need for changes concerning both the organisation and the structure of the supervisory authority to change the form of its activities and the way in which it is financed. It is necessary for one supervisory authority to acquire all statutory auditor services subject to professional standards for both public interest entities and entities other than public interest entities and to conduct administrative proceedings and disciplinary proceedings. This will contribute to maintaining the uniformity of proceedings, in particular as regards the finding of infringements and possible penalties. In view of the nature of the services provided by statutory auditors and audit firms and the fact that the performance of the statutory auditor's profession is in the general public interest, it was necessary to take appropriate action. The proposed solutions increase the operationality, efficiency and effectiveness of public oversight. They will ensure greater executive, operational decision-making as well as greater financial independence of the body necessary for the proper performance of supervisory tasks. Achievement of the intended purpose of the Act, i.e. The strengthening of public oversight of statutory auditors and audit firms should take place as of 1 January 2020, when the new institutional audit authority will fully carry out all the tasks entrusted to it. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u391	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u392	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Act on statutory auditors, audit firms and public oversight and some other laws in print No 3481. The aim of the Act is to strengthen public oversight of the activities of auditors and audit firms in Poland, which will contribute to enhancing the quality of services provided by these entities, including services related to capital market transactions. This will allow for a high quality of services provided by statutory auditors and audit firms, bring about the proper functioning of the capital market, increase the safety of trading in financial instruments and enhance the protection of investors, shareholders and bondholders. The role of auditors and audit firms in capital market transactions is enormous. With the services they provide, investors must be sure that the financial information contained in the tender documents, e.g. the prospectuses related to the issue of bonds or shares are true and reliable. Information related to the events and supervisory activities related to the activities of one of the listed companies - I mean here GetBack - which is a public-interest entity indicates that the current system of oversight of the quality of certification services provided by statutory auditors and audit firms, and especially in relation to capital market transactions, is failing. Currently, the Audit Supervisory Commission, which supervises the quality of services provided by statutory auditors and audit firms in units of public interest, does not have sufficient tools to control the quality of services provided by audit firms related to capital market transactions, as a significant proportion of the supervisory tasks have been delegated to the Polish Chamber of Auditors, which performs quality audits of statutory audits of entities other than units of public interest and carries out investigations and sanctions against them - I mean the national disciplinary officer and the National Disciplinary Court. The draft law presented by the government contains provisions establishing a separate body - the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency, in place of the Audit Supervisory Commission, which will be a state-owned legal entity forming part of the public finance sector, designated as the competent public authority supervising the audit in Poland. It will be equipped with new supervisory, investigation and sanctioning tools for statutory auditor services subject to professional standards performed both in public entities of interest and in non-public entities of interest. The Polish Chamber of Auditors in the new system will carry out tasks within the framework of public oversight to a limited extent. It shall continue to establish national standards for the pursuit of the profession, national standards for quality control and the ethics of auditors, as well as control of statutory auditors' compliance with mandatory vocational training obligations and the performance of statutory auditors' approval and registration tasks. Disciplinary proceedings for offences other than those arising in the performance of certification services and related services carried out in accordance with national standards for the pursuit of the profession by statutory auditors will also be under the responsibility of the Polish Chamber of Auditors. The powers of the Financial Supervision Commission will remain unchanged. In view of the importance of this law for the proper and safe functioning of the capital market in Poland, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports this law and proposes that it continue to act on it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-12-sejm-82-1.u398	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Club - the Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position on the government's print project No 3481. The bill is such a typical example of centralization of power. This time it is a proposal to centralise supervisory powers over the activities of auditors and audit firms in Poland. The project envisages the creation of a new state-owned legal entity supervised by the Minister of Finance under the name: Polish Audit Supervisory Agency. The new agency is to replace and take over its tasks as from 1 January 2020. The government justifies this centralisation with the need for greater control of audit firms and auditors because of their important role in capital market transactions. The Agency's task will be to control all audit firms by carrying out various forms of planned, ad hoc or thematic controls. Not only related activities will be covered by supervision, e.g. with audit or review of financial statements, but also all certification and related services provided by audit firms, in accordance with national standards for the pursuit of the profession. In addition, the Agency will have the power to conduct disciplinary proceedings against all auditors who have committed an offence and to refer applications to the court for their punishment. Similarly, the Agency's exclusive competence will also be to conduct administrative proceedings and impose penalties on all audit firms for offences committed by them in connection with the provision of certification and related services. The Agency will take over from the Polish Chamber of Auditors even keeping lists of audit firms. On the other hand, the Polish Chamber of Auditors will retain the right to establish national standards for the pursuit of the profession of statutory auditor, the principles of professional ethics and the control of professional improvement, as well as the approval and registration of auditors. The Board of Auditors will also conduct disciplinary proceedings but only against statutory auditors for offences other than those arising from the pursuit of the profession, e.g. arising from the provision of advice or service keeping of accounts. The role of the Polish Chamber of Auditors is definitely decreasing, and the government justifies that this professional self-government, i.e. the Polish Chamber of Auditors, did not take sufficient action on the tasks entrusted to it, and also points to the chronicity of proceedings conducted by the Chamber's authorities, i.e. the national disciplinary officer and the National Disciplinar Court. Such an approach of the government to improving, solving such issues, issues, is not a phenomenon. It is such a fundamental principle of this government: it is necessary to set up a new agency or foundation, to expand the administration, and it is necessarily central, and those controlled, in this case all entrepreneurs, employers, will pay for it anyway. The bodies of this new agency will be the president and the board of agencies, and the board will be composed of the president, deputy, seven members, two representatives of the Minister of Finance, one Minister of Justice, the Commission for Financial Supervision and the Polish Chamber of Auditors, employers' organisations and the Stock Exchange. There is no denying that the costs of this new unit will be borne by the lowest wagers, i.e. the controlled ones, the entrepreneurs. In our view, this project means the typical expansion of central administration at the expense of those who create the economy, i.e. entrepreneurs. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
